| D295 | 2026-11-14 | The durable queue for design jobs asked from Discord (WO-9.1, plan 7.12, [QA:V4], D243, D250, D251) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-9.1 review, 2026-11-14) on the placement, the one re-run and who may queue; the idempotency wording narrowed and amounts checked before queueing as review L6 and verdict 7 asked (below).** **Where:** in the core, not the bot (`gregtec.remote.jobs`, table `remote_job` in `gos.sqlite`, created by `gregtec.store`), so a job survives a restart of either process; the core's one worker thread (`gos-remote-jobs`, started in `server.run`) runs them oldest first. **Kinds:** `plan` (the Designer's own `plan_routes.designed`) and `power` (the Power view's `power_routes.designed`); specs are checked before queueing (`plan`: `target`, one of `rate`/`batch`, optional `within`, `tier`; `power`: `mode`, optional `tier`; text only, 200 characters each; `rate`, `batch`, `within` and `tier` are parsed with the designer's own `gregtec.planner.request` parsers and its rules, so a typo answers at once with the designer's reason instead of becoming a failed job). A job runs no model, so `/plan` and `/power` work in no-AI mode (plan 7.16). **Never completes twice (re-run once after a crash mid-run):** a job is keyed by (client, the Discord interaction id), so a repeated HTTP submit of the same interaction (a retried request whose answer was lost) returns the job already made (another spec under the same key is refused); the bot keeps no state across its own restart and never resubmits, and a new `/plan` from Clayton is a new interaction, so a new job; the worker claims with `UPDATE ... WHERE state = 'queued'` and finishes only a `running` job, and a `done` or `failed` job is never claimed again (two workers on one DB tested). **Judgement call: a job a dead core left `running` is queued again once** at the next start (its run never finished, so nothing of it was reported, and the designer saves the same ids for the same request on the same DB, D250); interrupted `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (2) times, it fails ("interrupted by core restarts; ask again") instead of crash-looping. **Delivery is at least once, the run exactly once:** the bot lists finished, undelivered jobs (`GET /api/jobs?undelivered=1`), posts each, then marks it (`POST /api/jobs/{id}/delivered`); a bot that dies between the two posts that result again, never re-runs it. A result the bot can't post (his DMs closed, a payload Discord refuses) never holds up the jobs after it: it is tried again after 30 s, then 60 s, and after 3 tries left alone with one short text-only notice until the bot restarts (it stays undelivered in the core; review M1). Finished jobs are kept 30 days. **Who may queue (judgement call, an exception to D251 like D165's credit decision and D206's persona):** admin and the client token `discord` only; a job writes *proposed* plans (D243, D250) and nothing else, while pinning, re-solving, accepting and moving plans stay the dashboards' and `gos`'s. A caller sees only its own jobs. **Vault:** each finished job is a journal line (`remote_j_<id>: plan; done; via discord; <target>; plan:<ids>`) through `bridge.ops.journal_to` (`VaultIO`), and the designer writes the plans' own vault notes as before. **Result** kept small: ids and the planner's own numbers per route/option (machines, EU/t, effort; net/gross EU/t, generator, fuel) in rank.py's order (D250), never re-ordered. | Implements plan 7.12's "queued designer job" and 8 row 9.1's queue; tests `tests/unit/remote/` (restart, crash mid-job, concurrency), the harness test `tests/integration/test_discord_harness.py` | WO-9.1 |
| D296 | 2026-11-14 | The bot as its own process: package, dependency, settings, secrets and the 10 s bound (WO-9.1, plan 4.2, 4.6, 7.12, D39, D77, D115, D117) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-9.1 review, 2026-11-14) on the package, discord.py as a default group (its `audioop` DeprecationWarning is harmless on 3.12; revisit on a move to 3.13) and route 1's PNG and BOM.** **Package (judgement call):** `core/gregtec/discordbot/` instead of Appendix B's separate `discord_bot/gregtec_discord/`: the bot reuses `gregtec.config`, `keys`, `applog` and `control` and runs from GOS_APP's one venv as `python -m gregtec.discordbot run --supervised`; only `adapter.py` imports discord.py, so the core never loads it, and `handlers.py` (the logic) is discord.py-free so the tests drive it with fakes. Appendix B updated. **Dependency:** `discord.py>=2.6.4` (MIT; brings aiohttp and its Apache-2.0/MIT deps) as a default uv group `discord`, so GOS_APP and CI get it; nothing vendored. **Supervised (D77):** `supervisor.discord_spec` starts it at login with the core (not with Play); exit 2 (`EXIT_SETUP`: no `[discord] owner_id`, no bot token, no core token, or Discord refused the token) leaves it stopped with the reason in `logs/discord.log`; it stops on the supervisor's `stop` line (D115). **Settings:** `[discord] owner_id` (required, a 17-20 digit snowflake), `guild_id` (optional: commands sync to his server at once, else globally), `dashboard_url` (optional; default `https://` + the first `*.ts.net` name in `[core] allowed_hosts`; must be https on a host the core accepts, so a plan link can't point elsewhere, D53, PM06); unknown keys refused. Ids live only in gos.toml. **Secrets (keyring):** the bot token `gregtec-os/discord` (the setup wizard or `gos key set discord`) and the bot's core token `gregtec-os/token-discord` (`gos token new discord`); `gos discord check` names what is missing, never values. **Discord surface:** no gateway intents (no message content, members or presences); the six commands carry `default_permissions(administrator)`; every message goes out with mentions off. **10 s bound ([QA:V4]):** every command defers at once (Discord's "thinking", inside its 3 s), and if the core hasn't answered by 8 s "Still working on it" is posted, then the answer; `/plan` and `/power` answer "queued" as soon as the job is stored. A follow-up goes to the interaction while its token lasts (14 of Discord's 15 minutes), otherwise (and after a bot restart, which forgets interactions) by DM to `owner_id`. A deep-lane handoff is polled every 3 s for up to 45 min and its result posted. **Phone link:** `<dashboard_url>/#/designer/<pl-id>` or `/#/power/<pw-id>` (the views' own hash routes); the PNG diagram and BOM attached are route 1's (rank.py's first), the others named with their `plan:` ids. | Implements plan 7.12's bot and 8 row 9.1's "answers within 10 s"; the from-his-phone check is a runbook step (`docs/runbooks/discord_bot.md`) | WO-9.1 |
| D297 | 2026-11-14 | Clayton only on every command and interaction; confirm buttons, alerts and the journal from Discord (WO-9.1, plan 0.1 principle 8, 4.6, [QA:V9], D59, D156, D220) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-9.1 review, 2026-11-14) on no response to anyone else and no cap approval from Discord.** An unexpected error in one of Clayton's own interactions gets a generic "something went wrong" reply (never to anyone else, review L1). Replies to commands are visible to the channel's members, so the runbook says to use a private server or channel (review L4). **One door:** every slash command, autocomplete, button, select and modal reaches `Bot.interaction`, whose first act compares the user id with `owner_id`; the command tree's `interaction_check` checks too, and the buttons' own view never runs a callback (presses go through `on_interaction` to the door). **Anyone else is dropped with no response at all** (judgement call: Discord then shows its generic "interaction failed", which says nothing about GOS; an ephemeral "not for you" would confirm the bot answers commands), no core call, no DM, no journal line, and a log line with neither the user nor the text. So no one else's text reaches `/api/ask` and no one else's "confirm" or button confirms as `discord` (D220). Discord text is data (principle 8): only Clayton's command options are relayed, as the user turn, and the bot reads no messages. **Confirm buttons:** after an answer, a pending action not yet offered is posted once with its read-back (D59), its expiry as a Discord timestamp, and Confirm/Cancel buttons (`gos:confirm:<id>`, `gos:cancel:<id>`); a press first removes the buttons from its message, then calls `POST /api/actions/{id}/confirm|cancel` as the client `discord`, so the action desk applies its own rules exactly as for the dashboard (the pending id only, one pending, 60 s) and its refusal text is shown as it is. **Not offered from Discord (judgement call):** approving a deep-lane task paused at its cap spends money (D156: paired devices and `gos` only), so the bot says to approve it on the dashboard or phone. **Alerts:** `/alerts on|off|quiet <1-24 h>` sets `kv` `discord.alerts` (`POST /api/discord/alerts`: a paired device, `gos` or the bot); quiet turns back to on when its hours end; WO-9.2's routing reads `remote.routes.alerts_state`. **Journal:** each answer, persona switch, alert change and button result is a line in the day's journal via `POST /api/discord/journal` (the bot's token only), folded to one line of at most 240 characters with `[[` defused, so Discord text can't add lines or links (plan 6.2). **Tests:** `tests/security/test_discord_foreign_users.py` drives every interaction kind with every command and button name from another user (dropped, nothing called) and the discord.py layer with duck-typed interactions; `tests/unit/discordbot/` covers each command, the 10 s bounds on a fake clock, buttons, delivery and restarts. | Implements plan 4.6 / 7.12's "answers only Clayton's user ID" and 8 row 9.1's "commands and clicks from other users are ignored" | WO-9.1 |
| D304 | 2026-11-14 | The 3D build guide: GuideNH's `exportsite`, framed; MrBruh's previewer the fallback (WO-10.1 spike, plan 7.10, D35, V15, R15) | **Pending Clayton (Lab export run).** The 3D view in the Build view is GuideNH 1.3.55's own `/guidenhc exportsite` output, run by Clayton in a Lab client with a game window and copied to `GOS_HOME/data/guide-site/` (judgement call: a fixed folder, no new config key). The core serves that folder unchanged under `/guide/` (`GET /guide/{path}`, `GET /api/guide`; admin, client or device credential; the dashboard's path guard) and the Build view shows one scene page in an iframe; it is **framed, never embedded**: embedding would loosen the dashboard's CSP (`base-uri`, inline styles) and pull GuideNH's tooltip templates and LGPL code into our page. Each guide file is sent with its own CSP that limits scripts, fetches, styles, images, fonts and media to `<origin>/guide/` (inline styles allowed, no inline scripts, no eval), `base-uri 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'self'`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`. That CSP limits which files the page loads (and blocks inline-script XSS); it is **not** an isolation boundary: the frame is same-origin with the dashboard, so a script from the folder can call `window.parent.fetch` (checked against the dashboard's `connect-src 'self'`) and reach `/api` with the paired device's cookie, read the parent's DOM and share its `localStorage` (review of WO-10.1, shown in a browser; the e2e test pins it). The trust boundary until WO-10.8 is the folder's contents: only Clayton's own unmodified Lab export goes there. **WO-10.8 must serve `/guide/` from its own origin** (a host name or port the `gos_device` cookie is never sent to) with its own credential (a short-lived signed path token, or loopback/tailnet only), not by dropping `allow-same-origin` (GuideNH's `app.js` reads `localStorage` unguarded and would throw), and caches the page scan. `.gz` files go out as bytes without `Content-Encoding` (the viewer gunzips them); the fixed type table also serves what a real export's `_res` can hold (`.bin`, `.jpg`, `.gif`, `.ttf`, `.otf`, `.mcmeta`, `.txt`), and the Build view opens a `gos/` guide page before the pack's. Licence: GuideNH is LGPL-3.0 (three.js MIT inside its bundle): GOS commits, copies, modifies and ships none of it; serving Clayton's own export at runtime is use, not vendoring. MrBruh's previewer (Apache-2.0) is the fallback if the Lab run fails (renders wrong, too big, or the guide page doesn't drive the export): re-implemented in the dashboard with three.js from npm and attribution, never his inline-CDN HTML. GuideNH's hover gives a position and a tooltip, not `registry@meta`, so WO-10.8's registry-ID hover, materials, checklist, BOM and hatch slots come from GOS's footprints (WO-10.2) by position, beside the frame. Not tried in the client harness: GuideNH's dev artifact would fit the dev runtime (GTNHLib 0.11.54, as ours), but the export is a chat command with no startup hook, so driving it needs shipped mod code or input automation (report §"Try in the client harness"). Report: `docs/reviews/WO-10.1_guide_spike.md`. | — | WO-10.1 spike; plan 7.10, 8 row 10.1; [G§1.2, §1.3, §6]; D35; D92; D158 (6) |

its kind puts it in a group, and `gregtec.alerts.policy.decide` (one pure function, the table the tests drive) picks its channels: **base** (WO-7.5's telemetry rules, WO-6.5's notices): the dashboard always (its cards are there already), **voice** while he is present and a voice client is connected, **in game** (a GregtecLink chat line, D76) while a game is connected, a **Discord DM** while he is away (D46) and only from `[notify] discord_min_level` (`warn`: crafting done and plan-buildable, `info`, are never DMed); **direct** (`credits`, D48, and `gos alerts test`): a DM whatever his presence, no voice or chat line (the dashboard banner exists); **away** (`game_paused`, `gos_offline`, D76): a DM only while he is away. **Away** = no keyboard or mouse input for 10 minutes (D76, fixed, not a setting): `gregtec.presence`, Windows' `GetLastInputInfo` against `GetTickCount` (modulo 2**32), behind an `IdleSource` interface with a fake for Linux CI (V35). **Judgement calls:** (1) **voice and a DM are one "he was told"**: an occurrence reaches him by at most one of them (said while present is not DMed when he walks away; DMed while away is not said when he returns); the dashboard and the in-game line are passive and always carry it; (2) **unknown presence is present** (not Windows, or the call fails): no away-only DM is ever sent on a guess; since that silently turns D46's AFK DMs off, `GET /api/alerts/routed` says `presence: unknown` and the health strip has a Presence item (`warn` on Windows, `off` elsewhere) (review verdict 2); (3) **quiet hours hold, never drop**: `[notify] quiet_hours` (local `HH:MM-HH:MM`, may wrap midnight; default none) holds voice and DMs below `quiet_min_level` (`err`), and Discord's `/alerts quiet <h>` holds DMs the same way; a held alert goes when the quiet ends if it is still active and the other conditions still hold; `/alerts off` stops every DM, the credit DM included (his explicit choice; the dashboard banner and the no-AI pause still act, D165); (4) **cooldown**: after a base alert's key went to a channel, a new occurrence of it (cleared and fired again) waits `cooldown_s` (1800) there; the credit DM is once per threshold per cycle and the D76 DMs once per pause or outage, with no cooldown; (5) failures on the health strip keep WO-8.1's in-game notices and are not routed (they are not alerts; D76's Discord case is "GOS offline", D300). `[notify]` is a service key (a bad value keeps the defaults and the core refuses to start); unknown keys are refused. All of it is configuration (when GOS speaks), not game numbers | Fills D281's "routing, quiet hours and dedupe across channels are WO-9.2's"; refines plan 7.12's Alerts | WO-9.2 |
| D299 | 2026-11-14 | The router in the core: occurrences, "no duplicate after a restart", grouping and the DM outbox (WO-9.2, D76, D165, D258, D267, D281, D295) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.alerts.router.AlertRouter`, ticked once a second right after the ops tick (alerts first), in its own try. **In:** `AlertEngine.sinks` (fired = active until cleared; key `<binding>/<rule>:<subject>`, occurrence its firing time), the notices' `deliver` (one-shot, deliverable 10 minutes; `power_deficit` `warn`, the rest `info`), and two polled sources: the credit watch's banner state (active while a threshold waits for an answer; key `credits:<pct>`, occurrence the cycle) and the watchers' paused state (D258: active while `paused_now`; key `game_paused:<instance>`, occurrence the pause's start). **Level-triggered (judgement call):** each tick offers every active alert to each channel it hasn't reached and asks `decide` again, so D76's "sent once, when both conditions are true, whichever comes second" needs no timers: the paused DM goes on the first tick that finds the game paused and him away, in either order, and Esc-then-walk-away DMs exactly 10 minutes after his last input. **Once:** `alert_route(channel, key, occ, ts)` in gos.sqlite records each delivery, so a restart never repeats one; the engine fires afresh after a restart (D281's `lost` rows), so the keys firing are kept in `kv` `alerts.routing` and a key that fires again is the same occurrence until `AlertEngine.settled` says its binding has been evaluated on fresh telemetry longer than the rule's hold plus 30 s (review L1, verdict 12: never wall time, so a game paused across a restart doesn't make it new). **Only through a bot (review M1):** a DM counts as sent when it is queued, so it is queued only while the `discord` client read the outbox in the last 30 s (`Context.discord_up`); otherwise Discord's verdict is "no Discord bot" and voice still says a base alert when he is back; a dropped DM was never posted, so it is no longer counted as sent and is offered again if its alert is active again. **Grouped:** base alerts due in one tick on one binding are one voice line, one chat line (the engine's `summary`: "Lab: power low and 2 machines stalled.") and one DM. **Voice:** with the router, the engine's `alert` push and the notices' `notice` push carry no `say` (the dashboards still get them); the router's line goes as WO-7.5's `alert`/`fired` shape to the voice client's sockets only, so the voice client is unchanged and nothing is said twice (WO-6.5's one-stall test still holds; without a router, as before). **Outbox:** `alert_dm` (text, buttons, the alerts it carries); `GET /api/alerts/outbox` (admin and the `discord` client: undelivered DMs plus `/alerts`' state) and `POST /api/alerts/outbox/{id}/delivered`; a DM whose alerts all ended before it was posted, or older than 6 h, is dropped, so a bot that was down never posts stale news (after a restart, once the first tick has read the sources, an alert neither active nor still adoptable counts as ended; an occurrence that comes back clears its "ended" mark; the in-memory history is pruned every minute, review L2); a pause whose start is unknown is new from the first poll that sees it (L5); a telemetry alert DMed while its binding is held says "(held: no fresh telemetry)" (L6); rows kept 30 days. `GET /api/alerts/routed` (admin, devices) shows the moment, each active alert and why a channel hasn't had it; `POST /api/alerts/test` (admin) queues one test DM; `gos alerts routed|test`. When the core hasn't answered for `[notify] offline_after_s` (60 s, more than the supervisor's ~2 s restart) and he is away (the bot's own input-idle source), it DMs once per outage, whichever came second; not when the supervisor's status table (the status token every managed process gets, D115) says the core is `stopped` (`gos stop`, D48's Stop), since he did that; `/alerts` off or quiet and quiet hours apply, from the last `/alerts` state the core gave. **Judgement call:** after an offline DM, "GOS is back" follows when the core answers again, so he knows whether to act, under the same `decide` as the offline DM (`/alerts` off or quiet, quiet hours, and only while he is still away; review L3). An outage that wasn't DMed yet is counted again from the bot's start, so a `down_since` left from before a stop is never days old. The outage (start, sent, the last `/alerts` state) is kept in `GOS_HOME\run\discord-alerts.json` (Clayton only), so a bot restart mid-outage doesn't send it twice. | D305 | 2026-11-14 | The footprint library: one footprint per (controller, tier channels, facing), computed from the structure table (WO-10.2, plan 7.10, D33, D123, D200) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.build.footprints`. **Stored nowhere** (judgement call): a footprint is a pure function of its `structure` row, a facing and the tier choices, so the structure table is the library and `fp.library(conn)` reads it read-only; nothing is written to `data/schematics/footprints/` until a consumer (the guide, WO-10.5's layout) needs files. **Key** `<registry>@<meta>\|<variant>[;<tier channel>=<value>...]\|<facing>`: the variant is WO-1.8's key (trigger stack plus the StructureLib channels the extractor set, e.g. a height); the tier channels are the row's `subs_json` channels that keep the shape (coil, glass, pipe, casing tiers), each at the value whose block is in the cells (the one the extractor built with); choosing another value swaps exactly those cells. A swap is by block: every cell holding the built tier block takes the chosen one, so a non-channel cell of the same block would swap too; none does at the pin (no block is a tier block of two channels in the real dump; the harness test fails if one ever is, and choosing two channels that swap the same block is refused). A channel whose cells hold more than one of its values (ambiguous) is left as built and out of the key, and choosing it is refused; a channel none of whose blocks is in the shape is left out. **Cells**: every cell's `registry@meta` (a numeric id anywhere makes the row unreadable and it is listed as skipped); a hatch cell carries its accepted `HatchElement` kinds; a hatch-only cell (GT `newAny`) has no block. Bounding box `lo`/`hi`/`size` over blocks and hatch cells, so north equals `size_x/y/z` (D123). **Rotation** in code about the vertical axis through the controller, a quarter turn clockwise seen from above per step north -> east -> south -> west ((x, z) -> (-z, x)); the facing is where the controller's front points. **Block metas are not rotated** (judgement call): GT casings, coils, glass and pipes carry no direction in their meta; a directional block would stand wrong when turned: one base-pack multi at the pin has one (`minecraft:anvil@1`, one cell; `minecraft:log@0` is vertical and safe), and addon captures may hold stairs or logs, so it is a question, not handled (a TODO in `Footprint.rotated` to flag or refuse them once a rotation table exists). Up/down facings are not made (plan 7.10 says four). **Legacy and new**: 2.9 keeps old controllers as separate MTEs (`MTELargeTurbineSteamLegacy` beside `MTELargeTurbineSteam`, `MTEDieselEngineLegacy` beside `MTELargeCombustionEngine`, `MTELargeBoiler*Legacy`), so each already has its own footprints; `lineage` is `legacy` for a class name ending in `Legacy` (every such class at the pin does, read from the GT jar's class list: 60+ legacy controllers in the real dump, mostly GT++ and bartworks machines such as `MTEIndustrialMixerLegacy`, not only turbines, LCEs and boilers) and `family` is turbine / lce / boiler from the class name, so reports keep them apart; lineage covers every legacy controller, family only those three. **Variant default** for `show` and lookups: the smallest by volume (D200's choice). Footprints are machinery: nothing here writes a schematic, and GOS never pastes one (D35). `gos footprints list\|show` | — | WO-10.2 PR; plan 7.10 "Footprint library"; [G][A§4.2] |
| D306 | 2026-11-14 | Lab captures of addon multis and the coverage report (WO-10.2, plan 7.10, 8 row 10.2, L1, D124, D238) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** **Capture format**: WO-10.4's `.schematic` reader is not on main (it is on `wo/10.4-schematic-io`, not merged in: not needed for this WO), so `gregtec.build.capture` reads GOS's own minimal `gos-capture` schema-1 JSON (controller block + meta, the facing its front had, trigger stack and StructureLib channels as set with `/sl_channel set`, the tool, cells `{p, block, meta}` in any frame, optional `hatches {p, kinds}`); `from_cells()` is the entry point WO-10.4's reader output will feed once merged, so a WorldEdit `//copy` or GuideNH region-wand capture becomes readable then. It takes the cells as the world or a `.schematic` holds them, the **controller's position** and a `{position: mID}` map read from the tile entities: a `gregtech:gt.blockmachines` cell's world meta is its tile entity's base type, not the machine, so each GT machine cell takes its tile entity's `mID` as its meta, and one without an `mID` is refused. **Hand-off to WO-10.4**: its reader must expose each tile entity's `mID` by position, and only a capture that kept its tile entities converts (WorldEdit `//copy` does). In a `gos-capture` file a GT machine cell's `meta` is already its `mID`. A capture is at most 64 cells a side (the box `MAX_CELLS` = 64³ assumes) and its controller is never `minecraft:air`. Cells are made controller-relative and turned to north; air is dropped; a `gregtech:gt.blockmachines` cell other than the controller is a placed hatch, kept as a hatch-only cell (kinds from `hatches`, else `Captured`) with the hatch as placed for reference; numeric ids refused; read once through `safe_open`. **Loading** (`gos footprints capture FILE`): one `structure` row with `source = 'lab-capture'` and a `structure_run` row (tool and file), replacing an earlier capture of the same controller and variant; **refused when the extractor's rows hold the controller** (captures are for what the extractor can't reach), and if a later dump reaches a captured controller the library keeps the extractor's rows and lists the capture as skipped. No new table (the structure table already keeps other sources, D123), so no schema bump. **Compare** (`gos footprints compare FILE`, L1's "cells match structure"): a capture of a base multi against its footprint at the tiers the capture holds; a hatch cell matches a placed hatch, its fallback block or nothing. **Coverage** (`gos footprints coverage`): a multi is needed when it is in a plan the ledger counts (accepted, building, running; production nodes and a power plan's `machines_used`) or in My base with a count; the machine catalog (`machine.is_multiblock`) is reported too; looked up by `gregtech:gt.blockmachines` + MTE id or the row's `machine_id` link; statuses footprint / capture / needs capture, with legacy and the extractor's caveats shown. "Machine facts" in the WO is read as the machine catalog WO-1.7's facts hang off (judgement call). Read-only | — | WO-10.2 PR; plan 7.10; 8 row 10.2; 9 L1 |
| D301 | 2026-11-18 | The phone's way in: Tailscale Serve to the core, the tailnet name, never Funnel (WO-9.3, plan 4.6, 7.12, D53, D117, PM06, V28) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** (1) **Setup is a command Clayton runs himself** from GOS_APP: `gos tailnet setup` (`gregtec.tailnet`) needs Tailscale running and logged in (`tailscale status --json`, its `Self.DNSName` is the name), reads the serve config (`tailscale serve status --json`), **refuses while Funnel is on for any port** (foreground sessions too) or while a raw TCP forward (`TCP[*].TCPForward`) reaches the core's port, and refuses to replace another handler on the PC's port 443, then runs `tailscale serve --bg --https=443 http://127.0.0.1:<[core] port>` and reads it back: the handler must proxy to the core and Funnel must still be off, else it removes the handler again and fails. It prints the name for `[core] allowed_hosts` and **never writes gos.toml** (the conservative choice: the tailnet name is accepted only once Clayton adds it, which is the WO's "only when configured"). `gos tailnet check` (read only) reports Tailscale running, Funnel off, no raw TCP forward to the core, the proxy in place and the name allowed (Tailscale's configuration only, not a request through it: the phone loading the page is the end-to-end check, runbook C.1); `gos tailnet off` removes the handler only when it proxies to the core, and leaves another service's handler on 443 alone (exit 1). A CLI call that times out (60 s; the real CLI waits after printing the admin-console link when Serve or HTTPS isn't enabled) shows the last lines it printed. `setup` and `off` refuse as gos-dev (they change Clayton's real Tailscale node); `check` reads. **No code path passes `funnel` to the CLI** (`Tailscale.run` refuses any such argument) and `--funnel` is refused with exit 3. (2) **The core refuses HTTP Funnel traffic itself:** `security.refusal` refuses any request or WebSocket with `Tailscale-Funnel-Request` (403 `funnel request`), whatever its Host or credential: Tailscale sets that header on every request that came through Funnel on its HTTP reverse-proxy path and strips any copy a client sends (`ipn/ipnlocal/serve.go`, `addTailscaleIdentityHeaders`, which deletes `Tailscale-Funnel-Request` and its `_` variants before setting it). A **TCP** Funnel (`tailscale funnel --tcp` or `--tls-terminated-tcp`) forwards bytes untouched, so the core can't tell it from the tailnet; `gos tailnet check` catches that one (Funnel on for its port, and a raw TCP forward to the core). The second layer covers HTTP Funnel only (review M1). (3) **What Tailscale sends (V28, from its docs and source, read 2026-11-18):** Serve shares a local service "within your tailnet", Funnel is the separate public command, proxies go to `http://127.0.0.1` (tailscale.com/kb/1242/tailscale-serve); "Tailscale Serve requires you to enable HTTPS certificates in your tailnet" (tailscale.com/kb/1312/serve); the proxied request keeps the browser's Host (`r.Out.Host = r.In.Host`) and adds `X-Forwarded-Host`, `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` and `X-Forwarded-For` (github.com/tailscale/tailscale `ipn/ipnlocal/serve.go`, BSD-3-Clause, read only to learn the behaviour, nothing vendored). So the core sees `Host: <pc>.<tailnet>.ts.net` (no port) and `Origin: https://<that name>`, which the existing D117 checks accept once the name is in `[core] allowed_hosts`, and refuse otherwise. (4) **`:443` entries:** a browser leaves out the default port, so an `allowed_hosts` entry written `<name>:443` (which the Discord bot's link already accepted, D296) now also allows the bare `<name>` (`Config.allowed_hosts`); `Config.tailnet_host` is the first `*.ts.net` entry, used by the bot's `dashboard_url` default. (5) **Not done, on purpose:** the identity headers (`Tailscale-User-Login`) aren't checked: tagged devices don't get them and the device cookie is the credential (D117); the core stays on loopback only (D115). Still open: the phone on cellular (L8), `docs/runbooks/phone_dashboard.md` E | Specifies plan 4.6's Tailscale Serve and "Funnel is never enabled" for WO-9.3; refines D117 (1) (the tailnet name added) and D296's `dashboard_url` default | WO-9.3 (branch `wo/9.3-phone-dashboard`) |

Tags in a fixed order and gzip without a timestamp, so the same shell gives the same bytes (a golden test pins them). **Judgement calls:** (1) **ids number registry names, metas share one:** plan 7.10's "palette 1-255" counts block types, as `BlockMapping` maps a name to an id and `Data` holds the meta; `gregtech:gt.blockcasings@1` and `@2` are one id. Names are sorted to get ids 1..N. (2) **Above 255 types the default is to split**, greedily by whole layers (y), a layer with more than 255 types by rows (z), a row by columns (x); a run of all-air layers (rows) next to a layer (row) of more than 255 types is folded into that layer's cuts, so no part places only air (review L2); every part keeps both readers right, and its `WEOffset` includes its corner, so all parts are pasted standing on the same block. `AddBlocks` (WorldEdit's nibble order, even cell in the low nibble, up to 4,095 types) only on request (`over="addblocks"`, `gos schematic sample --over addblocks`), and the notes say which and that Schematica-GTNH misreads that file. (3) **The shell rule** is `write_shell`, the only way GOS writes a shell: it runs WO-10.3's `palette.check` on every block state the shell uses (refusing air or `gregtech:gt.blockmachines` named, a block not in the target world's registry, a state not in the catalog, `hasTileEntity`, meta > 15) and refuses the whole shell on any refusal, with the palette report; an all-air shell is refused (empty palette). `palette.check` gets `max_types` (default 255, D308) and the writer passes None, since it counts names itself and splits; D308's 255-entry cap stays for `gos palette check`. (4) **meta > 15 stays out of shells** until worldedit-gtnh #30 is resolved: `write_shell` refuses it through the palette rule; the format layer `encode` still carries 16-bit metas (`AddData`) for files that aren't shells (footprint previews, Schematica ghosts of captures), and the round trip is tested to 0xFFFF. (5) **Pre-rotated** means the cells are written in world axes as they will stand (x east, z south, paste facing as designed) and pasted without `//rotate`; there is no rotation helper here: rotating a shell also rotates orientation metas, which needs the orientation families D308 (M3) asks the export for, so it belongs with WO-10.7's designer (a Question). (6) Nothing pastes from GOS: `gos schematic sample` writes to gos-dev's `GOS_HOME/data/schematics/shells/` (or `--out`) and Clayton copies the file into the Lab and pastes it himself; enforced in code (review M1): an `--out` (or any `-part-N` file) inside Play, Prism's instances folder, Export or Lab is refused with exit 2 by `doctor.touch_problem` (D223). **Licence:** re-implemented from the format as worldedit-gtnh v0.0.10 (`SchematicReader`/`SchematicWriter`, LGPL-3.0, tag `v0.0.10` = e5623ac) and Schematica 1.12.6-GTNH (`SchematicAlpha`, MIT, 3b03ee9) read and write it; their source was read to learn the behaviour, nothing copied. | Refines D308 (the 255 cap is the CLI's, not the writer's) | WO-10.4 PR |

For each GOS-written shell in `mod/harness/schematic/in/` (`tests/harness/schematic_inputs.py`, committed, a unit test keeps them current) it does what `//schem load`, `//paste`, `//copy` and `//schem save` do through WorldEdit's own classes, at "player" positions in the throwaway superflat world: the copy box is where **GOS** says the shell lands (player + the file's `WEOffset`, read from the NBT, not from WorldEdit), so a disagreement on the offset fails the copy; the harness also checks the world itself (each cell's registry name + meta against WorldEdit's reading, air above and below the box). `tests/integration/test_schematic_harness.py` reads the copies with GOS's reader (the dev world's ids through `BlockMapping`, remapped from GOS's compact ids) and requires every cell and the offset identical. **Judgement call:** the dev runtime has no EndlessIDs, so the harness covers ids below 4,096 and metas below 16 only; ids above 4,095 (`AddBlocks2`) and metas above 15 (`AddData`) are covered by unit tests against files laid out as worldedit-gtnh's writer and Schematica's writers lay them out (written in the tests from their behaviour, independent of GOS's writer), and in game only by the Lab round trip, a PC step (runbook `schematic_roundtrip.md`: paste, the offset by eye, `//copy`, `gos schematic compare`; and #30's `//set` of a meta-824 ore and a frame box copy to document meta > 15). | — | WO-10.4 PR |
| D311 | 2026-11-14 | Reading captures: writers, nibble orders, mappings and ids (WO-10.4, plan 7.10, [G§4.1-4.2], [E§5.1-5.2]) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `schematic.read` reads worldedit-gtnh `//copy` saves and Schematica-GTNH saves, gzip or plain, with every cell turned into registry name + meta as it is read (`Capture`: the schematic, the writer, how ids above 255 were stored, WEOrigin, tile-entity positions, entity count, notes). **Ids:** `Blocks`, plus `AddBlocks` bits 8-11 and `AddBlocks2` bits 12-15 (worldedit-gtnh, even cell in the low nibble), or Schematica's `Add` / extended-id `AddBlocks` (one byte per cell, taken when its length is the cell count and the file has more than 2 cells or is Schematica's); meta = `Data | AddData << 8`. **Judgement calls:** (1) **The writer** is worldedit-gtnh's family when the file has `WEOffset`/`WEOrigin`, `BlockMapping` or `AddBlocks2` (GOS's own files too), Schematica's when it has `SchematicaMapping`, `Icon` or `Add`, else unknown. (2) **Nibble order:** both orders are decoded; one *fits* when every id it gives is named by the file's mappings or the source world, id 0 counting as air even when neither lists it (review L3). The writer's order is taken when it fits (a note when both do); when only the other fits it is taken as *detected* with a note; both fitting with no writer known is refused ("refused rather than guess"), as is neither fitting with no writer known; a known writer's file that fits neither is read in its writer's order and refused by the ids left without a name (the more exact reason). A file with more than 65,535 distinct (id, meta) states is refused with a reason (review L1). `AddBlocks2` means worldedit-gtnh's order. (3) **Names:** `BlockMapping` and `SchematicaMapping` together (ids as unsigned shorts); an id with two names, or a key that isn't a registry name, is refused. A file without a mapping (MCEdit, an old WorldEdit) is named only from **the source world's `level.dat`** (`worldreg.read_block_ids`, `gos schematic read|compare --source-world`, read-only through `safe_open`): its `FML.ItemData` ids are used to translate the file's ids at read time and never kept (EndlessIDs; GOS still keys on names); an id nothing names refuses the file with the ids listed; id 0 is air. (4) Tile entities are listed by position only (a capture can hold them; a shell never does) and a missing `TileEntities` list is a note (worldedit-gtnh would refuse to load it). **Known limits (documented, not detectable):** Schematica's extended-id writer encodes an id that is a multiple of 256 wrongly (its loop leaves 256 in the low byte), so such a file reads as Schematica itself reads it, wrong where a wrong id is still named; Schematica's classic writer corrupts ids above 4,095 (an 8-bit high part ORed into a nibble), which the fit check refuses. **CLI:** `gos schematic sample PALETTE` (a test shell, after the palette rule, exit 0/1/2), `read FILE` and `compare EXPECTED ACTUAL` (cell by cell by name + meta; offsets reported; exit 0 identical, 1 different, 2 unreadable). | — | WO-10.4 PR |
| D314 | 2026-11-18 | The theme engine: note format, catalog check, versions and where themes live (WO-10.6, plan 7.10 "Themes (D36)", 6.2, 6.5, 7.0, D74, D206, D307, D308) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** All in `gregtec.build.themes` (D74; Appendix B's `build/themes.py`). **Format:** a `t: theme` note `th-<name>` in the plan 6.2 grammar: `name` (at most 40 characters), `v` (the version, set by code, never the model), `summary`, `fits` (line types, D315), `prompt` (the words it was made from; kept on every refinement), `change` (one line per refinement, `v<N> <what>`, the newest 12 kept), the rules `motif`, `massing`, `roof`, `window`, `lighting` (at most 8 lines each), `adapt` (`<line type>: <rule>`), `sample` (references to sample builds, free text for now) and `note`; the palette is the note's one ```` ```tsv ```` table `role block note`, each block `<registry name>@<meta>` (never a numeric id), each role one of wall, trim, pillar, floor, roof, window, light, accent, with at least one wall, roof, window and light entry, at most 32 rows. Text values are one line, at most 200 characters, with no `<`, `>`, tab or control character (theme text reaches a model only as tool data). These keys replace `*` in `_gos/schema.md`'s theme row. **Check:** every palette entry goes through WO-10.3's palette rule on the catalog alone (`palette.check` now takes `world=None`: a theme names no world, so the world-registry check stays the schematic writer's when a shell is written): refused when air or `gregtech:gt.blockmachines`, not a placeable catalog state (unknown meta), `has_te`, or meta above 15 (worldedit-gtnh #30); "listed twice" isn't a warning (a block may serve two roles). One refusal and nothing is written; the error lists each refused entry with its reason, so the deep lane can say it (the architect skill tells it to say the first refusal in its first sentence). No catalog: `theme_save` refuses ("run an export with the palette pass"). **Versions:** `themes/th-<name>.md` is the current version; each refinement first writes the current one to `themes/versions/th-<name>--v<N>.md` (`--` can't occur in an id), then the new one, numbered one above the current version and every kept one (so a theme made again after Clayton deletes its note continues its numbering, from review M1); a kept version is never overwritten with different text (a hand-lowered `v:` that collides is refused with the fix named), saves are serialised by a process lock, and a save re-reads the current note's `v` just before writing and refuses if another process changed it (review M2; a cross-process window of a few milliseconds remains). A refinement says what changed (`change`, required, at most 192 characters so `v<N> <change>` fits one 200-character line) and sends only the fields that change; the palette, when sent, is replaced whole. **Writes:** only through `VaultIO` with a new `theme` operation that only `gregtec.build.themes` uses: the guard refuses `write`/`append` on `themes/` (so `note_write` and the SDK's Write can't put an unchecked palette there; `note_write`'s description no longer lists `themes/`) and refuses `theme` anywhere but `themes/`, so no theme write can reach `me/` or `_gos/`. **Starter themes** (original, GT and vanilla blocks only): `th-foundry` (dark concrete, solid steel and heat-proof casings, reinforced glass; fits smelting, power, processing) and `th-marble-works` (GT marble and basalt, quartz pillars, glass and chemical grade glass; fits assembly, chemistry, general), authored in `vault-template/_gos/themes/` and deployed with `_gos/`, read from the repo's template first, else the live vault's `_gos/themes/` (as persona packs, D206); `_gos/themes/` joins `NOT_NOTES`. Refining a starter writes Clayton's copy at `themes/th-<id>.md` (v2, the shipped v1 kept in `versions/`), which then replaces the starter, as a pack in `personas/` replaces a shipped one. **Tools** (plan 7.0's `theme_get/list/save`): `theme_list` and `theme_get(id, v?)` read-only in both lanes (no DB needed); `theme_save` deep lane only, gated, with flat string arguments (the tool schema subset has no arrays: the palette one `<role> <block> [note]` a line, rules one a line, `adapt` as `<line type>: <rule>` lines, `fits` comma-separated), returning a `say:` line. The architect subagent's tool set is the read-only tools plus `theme_save` (a new `architect` kind in `deep_config.tool_sets`; the orchestrator has it too). The `architect` skill (`vault-template/_gos/skills/architect/`) covers themes; shells and build guides join it with WO-10.7/10.8. **Routing:** making or refining a theme ("make/refine/change/tweak/swap ... theme", "new theme", "theme ... swap/change") is a deep rule in the router; "what themes do I have" stays fast-lane (`theme_list`). **CLI:** `gos theme list|check [ID...]|select` (read-only; check exits 0/1/2). Nothing vendored; no new dependency | — | WO-10.6 PR |

| D340 | 2026-11-18 | CI's `mod` job runs the integration tests on 4 pytest-xdist workers, slowest files first, from one DB build per harness run (WO-CI-MOD, PM work order 2026-11-14) | The `mod` job's step `uv run --locked pytest tests/integration` took 43-52 min of the job's 60 (main `8a07a60`: 43:49, job 51:11, 235 passed, 21 skipped; `af55e84` was cancelled at 60 min). It now runs `-n 4 --dist loadfile --no-loadscope-reorder --durations=25`: **pytest-xdist** (MIT, a dev dependency; the runner's 4 vCPUs), one whole file per worker so each file's module fixtures are built once, as in a serial run. **One build per harness run:** the session fixture `built_home` (`tests/integration/conftest.py`, `harness_cache.py`) builds each game folder's GOS_HOME once (`collect.collect(..., 1, allow_harness=True)` + `build.build(..., allow_harness=True)`, as the fixtures did) into the run's shared temp folder under a `mkdir` lock, and copies it into each fixture's own folder (the build report's home path is rewritten to the copy's); a failed build is recorded (written aside, then renamed) so waiting workers fail with its message; a harness test checks a copy equals a fixture's own build (same files, same DB rows). Fixtures that check the build's own `Result` (`power_status == "ok"`, the crops and DB tests) still build themselves. **Order:** xdist's default puts files with the most tests first, so the 340 s single-test `test_power_view_harness.py` started last; under xdist only, `conftest.py` sorts the files of `harness_cache.SLOW_FIRST` (measured seconds) to the front, items within a file keep their order, every test still runs (local: 969 s to 693 s). Plain `uv run pytest` is unchanged (serial, collection order). Result on the PR, two runs: the step 13:26 and 17:48, the job 18:36 and 26:54, the same 235 passed and 21 skipped as main plus this PR's 8 cache tests. The `mod` job's `timeout-minutes`, which WO-10.4 had raised from 60 to 90, is 60 again. The Linux jobs run on `ubuntu-24.04`, no longer `ubuntu-latest`: on 2026-11-18 some runners got the 26.04 image, where `apt-get install lua5.2` fails (`Unable to locate package`, seen on PR #121's run), so the job names are now `checks (ubuntu-24.04)`. Audited for parallel safety: tests write only under their own `tmp_path`/`tmp_path_factory` (and the lock-guarded cache), read `mod/run/` without changing it, use no fixed ports, and depend on no other file's tests. `timeout-minutes` stays 60. **The `checks` jobs** (30 min limit): Ubuntu took about 7 min, Windows 19-29 min serially (29 is within 20% of the limit), so both now run `pytest -n 4 --dist loadfile -m "not serial"` and then, in a second step of the same job (run even if the first failed), `pytest -m serial` alone: every test still runs once on both OSes, and the two steps' passed + skipped equal a serial run's (a plain `uv run pytest` still runs everything serially; the marker only selects in CI). Under 4 workers Windows showed three load-sensitive failures; each was handled at its cause, not by retrying: (1) `test_with_play.py::test_the_control_port_and_the_hook_notice` (WinError 10053 instead of 403) was a real supervisor race: a refusal answered and closed the socket before the request body (sent apart by urllib) had been read, so the OS reset the connection and Windows dropped the answer; `_dispatch` now reads the body before any answer (`test_supervisor.py::test_a_refused_request_s_body_is_read_before_the_socket_closes` fails without the fix); (2) `test_oc_mailbox.py`'s four Windows hold tests held a file for a fixed 0.15-1.5 s, which a loaded runner could outlast before the core looked: the holder now lets go when the test says (after the poll, or, for the retry test, from the core's own retry sleep), so they are exact and stay parallel; (3) `test_oc_mailbox_stress.py::test_concurrent_writer_and_core` raised PermissionError opening outbox.tmp: on Windows the core's rename of outbox.json back to outbox.tmp holds the file an instant after outbox.json is gone, and the test's Lua stand-in raised where oc/mailbox.lua's `send` returns `nil, reason` (the agent sends the next seq next cycle, a gap the check already counts); the stand-in now does as the Lua does (counted as `oc_open_failures`), and the module is marked `serial` because its thread races are wall-clock bound. A fourth showed locally under 4 workers: `tests/unit/wiki/test_wiki_api.py::test_one_request_at_a_time_across_clients_and_threads` (max_in_flight 2) was a race in the test's fake wiki, which counted a request in flight until after its answer had been sent, so the next client's request could arrive first; the fake now counts it out before sending (a real overlap still counts 2). The real-keyboard-hook tests of `tests/unit/voice/test_ptt.py` (Windows only) are `serial` too: the hook is one per desktop, SendInput reaches every hook on it, and Windows drops a low-level hook that answers slowly. 10 tests carry the marker (6 run on Linux). | | PM work order WO-CI-MOD, PR #113 |
| D329 | 2026-11-14 | The addon watcher's question, notices and pipeline (WO-11.1, plan 5.2 step 4, 7.13, D69, D77) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-11.1 review, 2026-11-18) on the question file, first answer wins and admin only saying no; changed on a run that can't be loaded (verdict 4, below).** **Ask:** post-exit's "Mods changed. Export now?" is a file, `GOS_HOME\run\export-question.json` (`run\` gives gos-dev nothing, so only Clayton's processes ask or answer), written by the hook (`playhooks.ask_everywhere` → `packupdate/addons.ask`), which waits up to the 5 minutes of plan 4.2 for the **first** answer: the dashboard's approval card (new card kind `export`, buttons Export now / Not now, `POST /api/export/question/{id}/confirm|cancel` by id), the voice's plain "confirm" / "cancel" (only while no world action waits, so D59's action confirm keeps priority), or the Windows dialog shown at the same time (kept as the fallback when the core isn't running or the folder can't be written). As for actions (D59), the answer's `via` comes from who called; admin (`gos` commands) can say no, never yes; a cancel from a way in that can't confirm and that the asker can't name (in-game chat, the OC client, admin through `/api/ask`) is recorded `via: other`, never as a route it isn't; a model has no path to it. No answer is a no. **First answer wins** for real: answers are written under `run\export-question.lock` (`O_EXCL`; a lock older than 5 s was left by a process that died and is broken), so of two answers at once only the first is accepted (the second gets 409 or None), closing the question takes the same lock (no answer can write it back), and `ask` returns the answer written first even when the dialog answered too. **Notices:** the core (the dashboard's tick) says each once, `kv` `addons.announced`: one per stale episode (keyed by Play's fingerprint while it differs from the last export's; the WO-1.4 stale alert card stays while it holds) and one per question; the spoken lines hold no numbers (D267). **Pipeline:** `gos export run` now collects the recorded run and builds it into the DB **under the instance lock** (collect reads Export's folder, which only the lock keeps still), then the signature diff, curation tasks and flags (D330, D331); a run that can't be loaded (collect or build fails, for any reason) stays recorded with `"loaded": false` on its entry in the fingerprint state (`fingerprint.mark_loaded`), which keeps the stale flag up: the dashboard's stale card says the export isn't loaded, the core says it once (`unloaded:<run>`, its own spoken line), and the next post-exit, while Play still matches that export, asks "Last export not loaded. Load it now?" and on yes starts `gos export run --load-only` (collect and build again under the lock, then the diff, note and flags; no new export); Clayton can run that by hand too. Exit code stays 0 (the export itself worked). A failure after the build (diff, flags, report, note) reads "built <run> into the DB, but the diff note, curation tasks and plan flags weren't all written: ...", never "export failed". The pack-update playbook passes `load=False` and keeps its own collect, build and flag steps (D148). No new tables: `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 7. | — (implements D69's dashboard and voice ask, which WO-1.4 left to a dialog) | WO-11.1 PR |

| D332 | 2026-11-18 | Backups verified by a restore: manifests, the vault snapshot, `gos backup restore` (WO-11.2, plan 4.3, 6.6, 10 "Ops", D139, D142 (g)) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** (1) **Manifests.** The daily `backup` job (WO-2.5) now writes `gos-YYYYMMDD.sqlite.manifest.json` beside each DB backup: its size, SHA-256 and every table's row count (`sqlite_master` tables, FTS shadow tables included). **Order:** `VACUUM INTO` to `.tmp`, the manifest computed from the `.tmp` (counted, then hashed; nothing reopens it) and written (temp file, fsync, replace), then the `.tmp` renamed. A backup under its name therefore always has its manifest; a manifest whose file never arrived and a `.tmp` are removed by the next run; every DB backup made before this WO (today's and the older ones kept) gets its manifest when the job next runs (one that can't be read stays without, and its restore fails `no manifest`); a manifest write's own `.tmp` left by a kill is swept by the retention pass. (2) **The vault snapshot** (new): the same job zips the deployed vault to `vault-YYYYMMDD.zip`, `.git` included (history comes back), but never `atomic_write`'s `*.gos-tmp` temporaries, `*.tmp` files or git's `*.lock` files (a restored lock is a stuck lock), with a manifest of every file's size and SHA-256 and the zip's own hash; `me/` is in it (Clayton's notes are what most needs a backup; the zip stays in GOS_HOME, never leaves the PC). The session-end backup (D142 (g)) snapshots it too, under the same deadline (a snapshot cut short leaves nothing). Retention: `[maintenance] keep_backups` of each kind, manifests with them. The vault already had git (plan 4.6), but git in the vault folder doesn't survive the folder's loss or a bad `me/` edit, which git doesn't track. (3) **`gos backup list`** and **`gos backup restore`** (`gregtec.ops.backups`, `ops/cli.py`): `--verify` restores the newest DB and vault backups (`--date YYYYMMDD`: that day's; `--from DIR`: another folder) into a temporary folder and checks the DB file's hash, `PRAGMA integrity_check`, every table's row count against the manifest (a table missing or extra fails), the zip's hash, every entry's CRC, no entry outside the folder, and every file's hash, none missing or extra; it prints `ok`/`FAIL` per check and exits 1 with `RESTORE CHECK FAILED`. No fallback to an older day: a bad newest backup fails loudly and the message names `--date`. Without `--date` the newest DB and the newest vault zip can be from different days (a vault snapshot cut short): `--verify` notes it (`backup days`) and checks each; `--to` fails it, as the two are swapped in as a pair, and the message asks for `--date`. `--to DIR` restores into a new or empty folder that is not in or around GOS_HOME's `data`, `vault` or `backups` (the world backups), `[paths] prism_root`, `prism_instances` or `gos_app` (refused, exit 2) and keeps it; putting it in place is Clayton's manual step with GOS stopped (runbooks/ops.md §2), and a replaced vault needs `setup_gos_dev.ps1` again for gos-dev's read ACL (D82). Only reads GOS_HOME, so gos-dev may run it (D82). The name is the WO's own (`restore --verify`) rather than a `verify` subcommand. **Acceptance:** the backups made by WO-2.5's real job in a temporary GOS_HOME pass, and every corruption tested (flipped byte, truncation, row count, missing or unreadable manifest, damaged zip, changed/missing/extra file, `../` entry) fails; on a real harness DB too (`tests/integration/test_backup_restore_harness.py`, about 17 s in the mod job) | D139 (what the backup job writes) | WO-11.2 PR |
| D333 | 2026-11-18 | Crash recovery: what is tested, what was fixed, and the world-backup check (WO-11.2, plan 4.2, 4.6, 5.5, D75, D214, D295) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** (1) **Tests** (`tests/unit/ops/test_crash_recovery.py`): each writer runs in a child process killed hard (SIGKILL; TerminateProcess on Windows) after a seeded number of steps plus a seeded few milliseconds, three seeds each: the DB and `kv` (a row and its counter: both or neither; every reported commit kept; `integrity_check` ok; an immediate write gets the lock), the confirm flow (`ActionDesk` propose, confirm, take, result in a loop; after the kill the restarted desk leaves nothing proposed or confirmed, every action is accounted for, a failed or expired one says the core restarted, what went to OC stays sent or done and `take` sends nothing again), a repeated confirm (voice repeating "confirm", the restarted bot re-posting a press) is refused, the event store (a killed `record` loop, then the watchers' resend: each uid once, no orphan session), the vault (every note whole, checked by its own hash; the temp file of a cut-short write swept), the M1 reply (`inbox.json` whole or absent; a stale `inbox.tmp` no obstacle), the watchers' tails and the Discord bot's `alerts.json` (each old or new, never torn), and the real processes: a supervisor with the real core, killed hard; the core leaves on its own (stdin), a new supervisor brings it back on the same GOS_HOME, nothing re-queued, DB intact, run files right. The instance lock's kill test already exists (WO-1.4, `test_locks.py`). A process kill keeps the page cache, which a power cut doesn't: that half is the ordering test (each writer fsyncs before the rename that publishes the file) plus SQLite WAL with `synchronous=FULL` (now set on every `Store` connection, not left to the build's default, and asserted: the WAL is fsynced at each commit). **Scope:** the core and the supervisor are killed as whole processes; for the watchers, the bot and voice, their state writers are killed in a child process (the spool and event store, tails, `alerts.json`, the confirm path), not the whole process. (2) **Fixed:** `vault.io.sweep_tmp` removes `atomic_write`'s `.<name>.<8 hex>.gos-tmp` files left by a kill, once at the core's start (in `server.maintenance`, before anything writes; links not followed, `.git` and the vault's top-level `me/` left alone: GOS never writes in `me/`, so a temp file there isn't GOS's): notes were never torn, but the half-written temp files stayed for ever. The watchers' `tails.json` and the bot's `alerts.json` state were renamed without an fsync, so a power cut could publish an empty file: both now fsync first. Nothing else failed. (3) **Not re-engineered:** a confirmed action that wasn't sent when the core died fails at the restart (said and shown: "nothing ran"), as D220 built it, rather than being sent later: a stale world action is not sent without a fresh confirm. A DM posted but not marked is posted again (at least once, D295). (4) **The world-backup check** (`scripts/verify_world_backup.py`, `gregtec.ops.worldbackup`): Clayton runs it from GOS_APP (gos-dev can't read `backups\world`, D75); it copies the newest zip (or `--zip`) through `safe_open` (share-delete, retried 3 times, handle closed) into a scratch folder, checks every CRC, no entry outside the folder, `level.dat` (gzip'd NBT compound; the shortest path, so a world folder at the root or one level down) and every region file (`region/` and `DIM*/region/`: whole 4 KiB sectors, chunks inside the file, length and compression byte 1 or 2 right, each inflating to an NBT compound; empty region files allowed), then removes the scratch folder (`--keep` keeps it). It refuses a `--work` folder in or around `[paths] prism_instances`, `prism_root`, `gos_app`, the vault, `data` or the backups folder, and never writes elsewhere. It checks free space first (twice the zip for the copy, then twice the unpacked size), and a zip it can't read or copy (locked, access denied) is a `FAIL`, exit 1, not a traceback. NBT and region formats re-implemented from their published layout, no code taken from any project (licence: ours, MIT). Restoring a world over Play stays Clayton's manual, documented step (ops.md §4) | — | WO-11.2 PR |

| D312 | 2026-11-18 | The line layout planner and its layout JSON (WO-10.5, plan 7.10 "Shells" and "Placement", 7.1 step 9, D35, D200, D305) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.build.layout`, `gos layout PLAN_ID [PLAN_ID ...] [--option NAME=VALUE] [--json\|--card] [--out FILE]`, `GET /api/plans/{id}/layout[?power=<id>]`. **Input**: one or more saved plans (judgement call: a production plan and the power plan that feeds it are laid out together when both are named; nothing is guessed about which power plan feeds which line). Every node's machine x its count is placed (a power plan's generator node by its `machines` list, role `generator`); a multiblock's size is its footprint (WO-10.2) at its **largest variant by volume** (judgement call, reviewer's M1: D200's smallest suits WO-3.6's area estimate, but a keep-out must hold the machine as built, and the variant a recipe needs, a distillation tower's height say, isn't in the DB; the placement records `variant` and one warning per machine type names every variant with its size, to check before building), a single block (and a steam machine) is one block; a multi with no footprint is listed under `unplaced` with why (a Lab capture, D306) and never placed with a guessed size. **Geometry** (judgement calls, all options with these defaults): a west *hall* corridor with the one entrance in its west face; *bands* east of it, each a *spine* corridor along x with a row of machines on each side, every machine facing its spine (north row faces south, south row north); a row is at most `max_run` 64 blocks (4 chunks) before a new band; corridors 3 wide (walk past a placed item) and 3 high; a keep-out is the footprint's box plus `clearance` 1 on each side and `clearance_top` 1 above (D200's clearance), flush with the spine; overhead *chases* 2 high above every corridor (the pipe/cable trunk; a machine's runs drop through its keep-out ring; a machine 1 or 2 blocks high has its keep-out end below the chase, so its drops pass through corridor headroom, which WO-10.7 decides how to dress); boxes pairwise disjoint except that keep-outs touch. Order: the plans', then each plan's flow from upstream to downstream (Kahn, ties by node number, loop members last by number); each machine to the shorter row of its band. **Reach**: service space = corridors + keep-outs minus machine boxes; a 6-connected flood fill from the entrance; a machine is `ok` when the cell in front of its controller and a cell of each hatch kind its footprint takes (Maintenance included) open onto reached space; hatch cells with no face out of the box are counted `enclosed`; a multiblock whose footprint has no hatch cells is not checked for hatches and a warning says so (vertical movement in service space is Clayton's: ladder, scaffold, flight). **Logistics and power** (judgement call: plans carry no logistics blocks, so logistics are the flows routed in the chases): `services` lists each plan edge between placed machines (item or fluid, the plan's rate), the plan's boundary at the entrance `e1`, and EU per tier from the layout's generators of that tier; when the layout holds generators but none at a consumer tier, that tier is fed from them with `via_transformer: true` and a warning naming the `gos dist transformer` step (reviewer's M2), and `e1` (the base grid) only when the layout holds no generator; a plan edge to or from a node with no placement is not routed and a warning names it; battery buffers and transformers stay the ledger's (WO-4.5), not placed. **JSON** `gos-layout` v1 (`core/gregtec/build/layout_schema.json`): id `lo-` + 12 hex of the plans and the whole layout body (options and every placement's footprint key, variant and geometry), so a new capture or dump gives a new id, axes x east / y up / z south with y 0 the first layer above the floor and boxes lo/hi inclusive, `bounds` from [0,0,0], placements (footprint key, controller, facing, front, box, hatch cells with reached), keep-outs, corridors, chases, entrances, services, unplaced, warnings. Deterministic (no clock, no randomness). The plan's own `layout` stays WO-3.6's bounding-box estimate (changing it would change every plan's JSON and id): the corridor-aware layout is a separate, on-demand output. Nothing here writes a world or a schematic (D35); no GPL code read or vendored (own geometry). `gos layout` exits 1 when a machine is unplaced or unreached | — | WO-10.5 PR; plan 7.10; 8 row 10.5 |

| D316 | 2026-11-18 | The shell designer: input from the layout, the building vocabulary, the procedural generator and the outputs (WO-10.7, plan 7.10 "Shells", "Shell designer", "Placement", D33-D36, D73, D74, D158 (6)) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.build.shell` (engine), `shell_store` (make/save/rebuild/export), `shell_cli` (`gos shell design|list|show|export`), the deep-lane tool `build_request` (`agents/tools/shells.py`; the architect's and the orchestrator's) and `GET/POST /api/shells`. **Input** (judgement call: WO-10.0's SOW is not in the repo, so built from plan 7.10 as written): a WO-10.5 layout (on this branch: `origin/wo/10.5-layout-planner` merged in, not yet on main). Bounding volume = the layout's bounds grown by 1 block on every horizontal side (the wall ring) and `headroom` (default 4, 1-16) above its top (the roof); the volumes left empty = every keep-out (which holds its machine's footprint and service ring), corridor and chase, plus a **doorway** through the wall ring in front of each entrance (from its face out to the bounds, its height), plus **the layout's whole interior below the roof** (review M2, judgement call, the conservative reading of "keep-outs, corridors, chases": the layout's own bounds, `y` up to `wall_top`, are the machines' and their runs' space, so the shell places nothing there and its walls stand only in the 1-block margin ring; this is also WO-10.5's open "short machines' runs drop through corridor headroom (WO-10.7 decides how)": however those runs go, they stay inside the layout and the shell never obstructs them; cells above a short machine or between keep-outs are no longer fair game for a model `fill`); palette = the theme's swatches (WO-10.6's three modes via `themes.select`, blank mode uses `theme_uses.jsonl`, and a build with a `site` records its use) plus extra swatches (a prompt-only build must bring its own, at least wall and roof) minus excluded states; the **paste origin** (D318): the first entrance's `lo` cell; `max_blocks` default 20 000 (1-200 000); the prompt and line type. **Building vocabulary** (the model gets no code execution; it sends data): `walls`, `pillars`, `windows`, `roof` (flat or gable, edge, parapet), `lights`, `fill`, `frame`, `clear`, in layout coordinates, each naming a palette role (+ entry number) or one `registry@meta`; later operations overwrite earlier ones; at most 200 operations; an operation (or the running design) far above `max_blocks` is refused before it fills memory. **Procedural generator** (the fallback, the No-AI path for the CLI and the Designer's "Design a shell around it" button, and the test baseline): walls with a trim plinth, an accent eaves band, windows (high strip / tall bays / mid band from the theme's window rules), pillars at corners and every 8, a flat roof with the roof role's last entry as edge (gable when the roof or massing rules say steep/gable/pitched and the short side is at most 33), a parapet when the rules say so, a light grid every 6 in the roof, and the doorways cleared. **Outputs**: `data/build/shells/<sh-id>/` with `design.json` (input, operations, status, repairs, notes, counts), `layout.json`, `palette.tsv` (role, block, name, count), `preview.json` (the exposed faces merged into rectangles) and `notes.txt` (with the paste steps); the id is a hash of layout, palette, options and operations. The dashboard's shell card (new catalog card `shell`) draws an isometric preview from the faces in role colours (design tokens; no textures), the manifest and the paste steps; the Build view lists the builds. Licence: nothing vendored; the schematic is WO-10.4's own writer. Plan 7.0's tool name `build_request(spec)` kept. `build_request` takes `save` (default true): `save` false checks a design and returns its operations without saving, the architect's first look at the procedural ops (review L3), so an architect shell leaves one build, not two. The router's shell rule leaves "how do I make/craft ... shell" to the fast lane's recipe lookup (review L4). Reviewer (advisor) agreed with the input, ring, headroom, doorways, theme plus extras, vocabulary and file location, with M2's interior rule added | — | WO-10.7 PR; plan 7.10; 8 row 10.7 |
| D317 | 2026-11-18 | The shell gate: what is repaired and what is refused (WO-10.7, plan 7.10, 8 row 10.7) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** Every design (model or procedural) goes through `shell.design`. **Refused, nothing written**: a malformed operation or unknown key, a role the palette doesn't have, any state outside the build's palette (the palette itself passed WO-10.3's rule on the catalog first, so a tile-entity block, `gregtech:gt.blockmachines`, a meta above 15 or a state not in the catalog is never in it, and the refusal quotes the rule's reason), more than `max_blocks`, no blocks, or **more than 25% of the design's placed cells** outside the bounding volume or inside a keep-out, corridor, chase, doorway or the layout's interior (too far off to repair: redesign). The share counts only cells an operation **places** a block in: a `clear` places air, so a clear reaching past the bounds is neither counted nor reported as a repair (review M1; before, a no-op clear west of the shell refused a valid design). **Repaired**: below that share, the offending cells are removed (judgement call, the conservative repair: removing decoration can never put a block where it mustn't be; replacing or moving blocks could), listed as `repaired: removed N cells inside keepout k3`, the build marked `repaired`. The repaired cells are inspected again and must be clean, and the palette rule runs once more on the states actually used. Export (`gos shell export`) first lays the build's saved plans out again and refuses when the layout id (a hash of the geometry, WO-10.5) differs from the saved one, or a plan is gone: after a re-solve or new footprint data the 3D guide and the floor plan show the new keep-outs, so the shell must be designed again (review M3). It then rebuilds the design from its files, runs the whole gate again and applies the palette rule against the **Lab** world's `level.dat` registry through WO-10.4's `write_shell`; a design changed on disk or a catalog that no longer has a state is refused there | — | WO-10.7 PR; plan 7.10; 8 row 10.7 |

| D319 | 2026-11-18 | The 3D build guide: GOS's own SVG viewer of the footprint beside GuideNH's export, and the export on its own origin with a path token (WO-10.8, plan 7.10 "3D build guide" and "Viewer features", 7.14, D35, D304, L6) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** **Viewer (judgement call):** the rotate/zoom/hover/layers/materials/checklist/hatch-slot/BOM view is GOS's own, drawn in the dashboard as SVG (`dashboard/src/views/guideMesh.ts`, `BuildGuide.tsx`) from the core's footprint data (`gregtec.build.guide_data`: positions from WO-10.2's footprints, names and the top/side faces' average colours from WO-10.3's catalog; never a numeric id, never GuideNH's hover payload, D304). Unit cubes drawn far to near by their centres' depth in an orthographic view the viewer turns (yaw, drag or 45° buttons) and tilts (pitch, drag), only faces that look at the viewer with no block in front; zoom by wheel or buttons; layer slider shows the structure up to a layer; hatch-only cells dashed, hatch-slot cells outlined in the accent colour; the controller's front face outlined, an arrow out of it and an N/E/S/W compass that turns and tilts with the drawing, so a turned view can't be built mirrored (review M1; each layer's panel counts its hatch slots and, of those, the hatch-only cells, as `hatch_slots`/`hatch_only`, review L1); hover (or a tap on the phone) names the block, its `registry@meta`, its layer and offset from the controller and the hatch kinds it takes. **No new dependency**: D304 chose GuideNH's export (framed) for the in-game look and three.js only as the fallback if the Lab run fails; an SVG of a multiblock (a few thousand faces at most) needs neither WebGL nor a 600 kB bundle, works in jsdom/vitest and headless Chromium, and the GuideNH frame stays beside it for real textures. Block colours are the catalog's averages (plan 7.10's third fallback); no texture or export file is committed or copied, both stay in `GOS_HOME/data`. **Own origin (D304's requirement):** `/guide/` is served only on the guide host `guide-<dashboard_host>:<port>` (derived, no new config key: `Config.guide_host`), never on the dashboard's host; the dashboard's `gos_device` cookie is host-only so never goes there, and the guide host is not in `allowed_hosts`, so the core refuses an `/api` request carrying its Origin (and `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or `same-site`). The guide host is a **sibling** name, never a child (review H1): a script on `guide.gos.localhost` could write `gos_device` with `domain=gos.localhost` and the dashboard would be sent both cookies (Starlette keeps the last), while `guide-gos.localhost` can only set cookies for itself (`localhost` is no cookie domain; checked in Chromium, e2e). Defence in depth: the core refuses any request carrying `gos_device` more than once ("repeated device cookie"), as it refuses a conflicting Host. On the guide host `HostOriginGuard` allows only GET/HEAD under `/guide/`, no WebSocket, an Origin only its own, never Funnel, and from another site only a frame's navigation (`Sec-Fetch-Dest: iframe`, `Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate`: the dashboard framing it; a top-level `document` navigation from another site is refused, since the dashboard never opens the guide in a tab). **Credential:** a path token `/guide/<expiry>-<HMAC-SHA256>/...` (key made at core start, in memory only; 6 h, a play session, judgement call), issued by `GET /api/guide` to a paired dashboard on the PC; the Build view asks for a new one 30 min before expiry, and whenever its socket opens again after dropping (a core restart makes a new key, so every older token is refused; review L2). Relative links in the export stay under the token. The guide files' CSP is `frame-ancestors http://<dashboard_host>:<port>` (no `X-Frame-Options`: `SAMEORIGIN` would refuse the dashboard, now another origin) plus `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`; the dashboard page gets `frame-src http://<guide host>`. The iframe keeps `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin"` (GuideNH's `app.js` needs its own `localStorage`), which is now the guide origin's, not the dashboard's. The e2e test that pinned `window.parent.fetch('/api/state') == 200` now pins it blocked. **Phone**: the tailnet name has one host name (Tailscale Serve), so no guide host there: `/api/guide` gives `frame: null` and the phone gets GOS's own viewer only (judgement call). **Scan cache:** `guide.ScanCache` re-parses a page only when its size or mtime changed (each call still lists and stats the folder, so a replaced export shows at once). **Opened from** the Build view's picker, the Designer's machine nodes and My base's machine rows ("Build guide" when the mte id has a footprint), as `#/build?guide=<registry name>@<meta>[&variant&facing&tier=<ch>=<v>]`. Built to the design system; **needs Clayton's board approval** (D92, D158 (5)). **Licence:** nothing of GuideNH (LGPL-3.0) or MrBruh's previewer (Apache-2.0) read into or copied into the code; the projection is re-implemented from first principles. | D350 | 2026-11-19 | A Lab capture saved as a `.schematic` becomes a footprint: what the reader keeps, how the controller, its facing and the hatch kinds are found (WO-10.9, G10 defect 1, plan 7.10 "Footprint library", 8 rows 10.2 and 10.4, L1) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** **Reader:** `schematic.read` keeps each tile entity as `TileEntity(pos, id, mte_id, facing, rotation, flip)`: its cell (none when the tag lacks an integer `x`, `y` or `z`), its registry id string (`id`, e.g. `BaseMetaTileEntity`) and, when they are integer tags, GT's `mID` and `mFacing` and StructureLib's `eRotation` and `eFlip` (bytes `MTEEnhancedMultiBlockBase.saveNBTData` writes into the same compound); everything else in the tag is dropped. Key names and types checked against GT 5.09.54.265's bytecode (`BaseMetaTileEntity.writeToNBT`: `setInteger("mID")`, `setShort("mFacing")`, ForgeDirection's ordinal) and against the worldedit-gtnh harness's own save (4 `BaseMetaTileEntity` with `mID`; the EBF controller's world meta is 2 and the hatches' 0, so the world meta is not the machine). No numeric block id is a key anywhere: cells stay registry name + meta, a GT machine cell's meta becomes its `mID`. **Command:** `gos footprints capture|compare FILE` reads either kind, told apart by content (a JSON object or gzipped/plain NBT), never by extension; options `--controller x,y,z`, `--facing`, `--trigger`, `--channel`, `--source-world` are for a `.schematic` and refused with a `gos-capture` file. **Controller (judgement call):** the one GT tile entity whose `mID` the DB's `machine` table marks `is_multiblock`; none or several is refused naming the candidates, unless `--controller` names the cell (box coordinates, as `gos schematic read` lists them); without a DB, `--controller` is required. Any other GT machine in the box is a placed hatch. **Facing (judgement call):** the controller's saved `mFacing` (2 north, 3 south, 4 west, 5 east); `--facing` only when the file saved none or a vertical one, and a `--facing` that disagrees with the saved one is refused (a wrong cell named, most likely). **Hatch kinds (judgement call):** a placed hatch whose `machine.class_name` is exactly one of the base classes of the 8 of GT `HatchElement`'s 15 elements at the pin that have a single base class in `implementations` (`MTEHatchInputBus` -> `InputBus`, `MTEHatchOutputBus`, `MTEHatchInput`, `MTEHatchOutput`, `MTEHatchEnergy`, `MTEHatchDynamo`, `MTEHatchMaintenance`, `MTEHatchMuffler`) takes that kind; any other class (subclasses such as ME buses, TecTech and GT++ hatches, and the other 7 elements such as `ExoticEnergy`, `MultiAmpEnergy` or `LaserSource`: the DB has no class tree) stays `Captured`, as the JSON path's unnamed hatch. The cells then go through `capture.from_cells`, as the JSON path's do (unchanged; regression-tested). **Proof:** the schematic harness places an EBF with three hatches as a player does, `//copy` + `//schem save` with WorldEdit's classes; `test_footprints_schematic_harness.py` turns the save into its footprint through the CLI and `coverage` reports it `capture` (adds < 1 s to the run). **Licence:** GT5-Unofficial (LGPL-3.0) read as bytecode for two key names only; worldedit-gtnh (GPL) stays a harness-only runtime dependency as in D310; nothing vendored, the readers are re-implemented from the formats | D306 (its hand-off of the `.schematic` path to WO-10.4); D311 (tile entities kept by position only) | WO-10.9 PR; G10 review defect 1; plan 7.10; 8 rows 10.2, 10.4 |
| D351 | 2026-11-19 | What a `.schematic` capture refuses (WO-10.9, plan 7.10, D306, D350) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** Everything the JSON path refuses, through the same `from_cells`: a box over 64 a side (after air is dropped), a capture of only air, a block not named by registry name (an id without a `BlockMapping`/`SchematicaMapping` name and no `--source-world`: refused by `schematic.read`, D311), a meta or `mID` outside 0..32767, and a multi the extractor's dump already holds (`load`).
