| D352 | 2026-11-19 | What the planner caches between designs, its key, its bounds and when it is dropped (WO-3.9, G3 finding 5, D196) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.planner.cache`, in the core's process (also the CLI's, for one command). **Cached:** (1) WO-3.3's batch reader (`graph._Reader`: items, fluids, ore slots, recipes, makers, uses, members, research, a map's machines), one per (DB file, DB generation, export run), held by one build at a time (its lock); a build that fails part way drops it; one past 250 000 recipes is dropped after its build; at most 4. (2) D196's component solves (`effort.Effort._solve`: a component's graph, sized machines and flow, or "none"), keyed by the DB file, its generation, the run id, the component request's digest (key, tier, machines, boundary, constraints), My base (its tier, and which owned machines, stock entries and line outputs are positive: all the graph boundaries and the machine check read, so an edited quantity reuses the solves; review MEDIUM-1) and a digest of the chain templates and `raw_inputs.tsv`, everything the solve reads; least recently used of 512 dropped (about 0.5 MB each on the client harness DB, so a few hundred MB at most; the reviewer measured 213-309 MB peaks after 83-261 entries; review MEDIUM-1). The shared reader resolves a key in full once (`key_ids`, in id order), so two items sharing an 8-character NBT prefix never depend on what an earlier design read (review LOW-6). Graphs of the design's own target, sizing, metrics, ranking and saved plans are not cached. **DB generation (judgement call):** a digest of the file's identity (device, inode), every `export_run` row (id, run key, created, fingerprint, sources), a `kv` row `db.build`, a counter `gos db build` increments in its transaction (1 in a fresh DB, so two fresh builds of the same exports stay row-identical for D340's harness cache; n+1 on every rebuild into the same file, review HIGH-1; so a rebuild of the same run, WO-1.9's pack update and WO-11.1's addon export, which all build, are new generations), and the `structure`/`structure_run` counts and largest rowids (WO-1.8's loader and WO-10.9's captures change footprints without a build). Writes the planner doesn't read (events, plans, other `kv` rows) keep it. A changed generation drops every entry of that file; an in-memory DB is never cached; `GOS_PLANNER_CACHE=0` turns it off. Proof of no change: `tests/unit/planner/test_cache.py` designs 5 production requests and the 4 power modes with the cache off and on, byte-equal (plans, ranks, cited ids), plus every key input varied; `tests/integration/test_design_cache_harness.py` does it on the harness DB (G3 steel and the power modes) and checks a rebuild drops it. Measured on the client harness DB (4 shared CPUs, `scripts/design_timing.py`, origin/main 045eeb8 before): Designer steel ingot HV 74.0 s / 70.7 s before (first / second run in one process), 27.3 s cold / 1.5 s warm after; power best LV 97.4 / 110.6 s before, 31.0 / 9.5 s after; self_sustaining LV 3 options 219.6 / 225.0 s before, 37.2 / 21.4 s after; every case's plan ids identical before and after. The self-sustaining case also needed one query fixed, not cached: `power._fills` (is an item a container of the fuel) compared a computed item key row by row over every recipe of the run (0.135 s a call, 1115 calls, 150 s of the 165 s left); it now resolves the keys to ids first and runs on the output and input indexes, the same answer on 301 of 301 checked pairs (real filling pairs and random ones), about 450 times faster. Tests reset the cache in `tests/conftest.py` (autouse) | — | WO-3.9 PR; G3 review finding 5; D196 |
| D353 | 2026-11-19 | Designs as jobs: stages, one per kind, what Cancel guarantees (WO-3.9, plan 7.1, 7.14) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.planner.design_jobs`, `gregtec.planner.progress`, `gregtec.api.job_routes`. `POST /api/designer/jobs` (`{spec}`, `{resolve}` or `{pin}`) and `POST /api/power/jobs` check the request first (400/404, or the clarifying question with no job), then answer at once with the job; it runs on its own thread; its stage, k of n, elapsed time and Cancel reach the views and the right rail by the `job` WebSocket push (at most 4 a second; a change the throttle holds back is pushed once it is up, review LOW-1) and the state's `jobs`, and the views also poll `GET /api/design-jobs/{id}` each second. Elapsed time is the core's `elapsed_s` plus the time since this device received it, never the core's start against the device's clock (review LOW-3); "k of n" never passes n (review LOW-2). **One job per kind** (Designer, power); a second is a 409 naming it, never a queue; both kinds share the planner's lock (`designer._LOCK`, now re-entrant), so the second waits as "waiting" and can be cancelled there. **Cancel** is checked at every `progress.stage`/`check`: between stages, before every staged MILP call (`solve._run`), per item the graph builder expands and per component of the effort walk; all of these come before `saving`, so a cancelled job wrote no plan JSON, row, vault note or `designer.json`/`power.json`, and designs never touch the ledger (accepting does). From `saving` on, Cancel is refused (greyed) and the job ends done. A cancel mid-MILP waits for that call (one HiGHS solve). **Callers kept (judgement call):** `POST /api/designer/design` and `/api/power/design` still block until done (WO-11.3's rehearsal, scripts); the agent tools (`design_production`, `design_power`), voice (through the agents), the Discord queue (`remote.jobs`, durable, sequential) and `gos plan`/`gos power design` call the same functions unchanged, with the cache; only the dashboard moved to jobs (designs, re-solves, pins; accept and rename stay quick calls). The core's loop stays responsive (the job is a thread; tested with the API answering during a held job) | — | WO-3.9 PR; plan 7.1, 7.14 |

| D355 | 2026-11-19 | The core reads a request's body before it answers, refusals included (Windows reset fix; D340's supervisor fix applied to the core) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** `tests/unit/voice/test_core_client.py::test_ask_errors_say_whether_the_core_answered` failed twice on Windows CI (WinError 10053, then 10054 on `fe3641b`, run 44460564006): a wrong token's `POST /api/ask` was answered 401 and closed while urllib's body (sent in a second `send`) was unread or still on its way, the OS reset the connection, and Windows dropped the answer, so the voice client said "Core offline." when the problem was the token. Reproduced on Linux: the 401 came before the body, and sending the body then got `ECONNRESET` (Linux keeps the answer readable, Windows does not). **Fix:** an outermost ASGI middleware, `security.ReadBodyFirst`, reads what is left of the body before any answer starts (the Host/Origin guard's 403, 401/403 credentials, 413/415, 404/405, 503): at most `app.READ_FIRST_MAX` bytes (the largest body a route takes, the screen frame's 12 MiB, as the supervisor bounds it by its own `MAX_BODY`) and for at most `security.READ_FIRST_S` (2 s), then the same answer as before. The rest is dropped, never parsed or logged. Not read: a declared length over the bound (answered at once, as before), a request without a body, and one with `Expect: 100-continue` (its body isn't sent yet). A route that read its body is not delayed. Clients are unchanged: `control.call` (voice, `gos`, Discord, watchers) rightly calls a reset "no answer"; there is no client retry. Tests: `tests/security/test_core_refusal_body.py` (every refusing path waits for the body on a live core and fails without the fix; a body that never comes holds a refusal only for the timeout while the core serves others; an oversized one isn't read; the middleware's bound, timeout, disconnect and pass-through, socket-free) | Refines D340 | PM work order 2026-11-19 (Windows CI run 44460564006) |

| D362 | 2026-11-19 | The hover-item eval: what a case is, what the scorer checks, why these 10 items, and "this" in a question takes the pointed-at item (WO-8.4, plan 9, 7.8, D286, D337 F3, G8 should-do 1, G11 finding 2) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** (1) **Case** (`tests/evals/hover_items.tsv`, `gos eval hover`): the hovered item exactly as GregtecLink's client module reports it (`target` `item` or `block`, registry name, meta, NBT in the export's typing; never a numeric id), the question said while pointing, and one expectation: `name`, `make`, `use`, `tier`, `machine` or `none`. The file holds no game number and no `r:`/`mte:` id (a unit test checks): the item, its rows and the machine's `mte:` are read from the DB in use at run time by stable keys (the recipe eval's `Ground` SQL), so the same file runs on the client harness DB and on his full pack's. A case whose item, or NBT variant, the DB lacks is reported `not in this DB` and fails the run; the paid run checks every case first and spends nothing then. (2) **Scorer** (deterministic, unit-tested with mutations): fails a reply that asks back, is about no item or another one than the hovered variant (the reply's resolved key, which the card shows), doesn't name the item by the DB's name, cites an id the DB lacks or a row that neither makes nor takes the item (a variant's plain item's rows are allowed, as "what's this?" names them, D286 LOW-6), states a number no cited row backs (counts of the item's recipes allowed), or misses its expectation (`tier`: one of its lowest-tier rows cited and that row's tier named, no other; no tier named when that row has none). (3) **The 10 items** (all resolve on the client harness export, checked by `tests/integration/test_hover_eval_harness.py`, and all are ordinary GTNH 2.9 items): GT Steel Ingot ("what's this?"), LV Motor ("how do I make this?"), Iron Ingot ("what is this used for?"), GT++ Hot Arcanite Ingot ("which tier is this?": its only recipe is tiered, where most ingots also smelt with no tier), IC2 Iron Plate (ambiguous: GT's plate has the same name and wins a lookup by name), a charged RE-Battery (an item with NBT variants), IC2's Universal Fluid Cell of water (a fluid cell with NBT; its uses are that variant's own), the Debug Fluid Tank (no recipe makes or takes it, in the harness and in any pack: it is creative-only), Good Generator's Field Restriction Casing (the addon item: **judgement call**, none of the four pack addons of plan 3 is in the client harness, so "addon" is a GT addon mod GTNH ships), and a looked-at Electric Blast Furnace (a block, cited by `mte:`). (4) **Finding F1, fixed in code (no prompt or skill changed):** before this, only "what's this?" used the hover; "how do I make this?", "what is this used for?" and "which tier is this?" were routed with the subject "this", which matches no item, so the hover eval's fake run failed 7 of 10. The asker now asks the game link for the pointed-at item when a fast-lane or instant item question's subject is "this"/"that"/"it" (with "one", "item", "block", "thing"), or when a subjectless question ends on "this"/"that" (with "one", "item", "block", "thing": "which tier is this", "what tier is that one"; review M1: "this" inside a question, as in "is this a good time to build a base", is not a pointer and probes nothing), and uses the DB key of what "what's this?" would resolve as the subject (`whatsthis.resolve`, the exact NBT variant first); with nothing pointed at (or an item the DB lacks) a "this" question gets "what's this?"'s sentence; a question that names its item is never probed; with no game link nothing changes. (5) **Runs**: `--fake` (CI: unit tests on the tool tests' DB, and all 10 in the mod job's integration tests) answers with an oracle fast-lane model that reads only the pre-retrieved results; the real run (fast lane, `llm_usage` task `eval:hover`) prints its worst case first (about $0.15 on the harness DB: every case at 3 calls plus the check's retry, no cache), refuses while D48's pause or his AI switch is on or when today's spend plus that estimate would pass `[budget] day_max_usd`, waits for `yes`, and stops at the estimate. **The day-cap refusal refines D334 for paid evals** (D334's day cap only tells, nothing pauses): an eval is a run he starts and can move, so it refuses up front rather than cross the cap mid-run; on the sitting he raises `day_max_usd` first (checklist 3.5's note, 3.14.5) or runs it another day. The harness test also asserts each case's DB name and mod, so a changed meta on his pack fails loudly | — | WO-8.4 PR |
| D363 | 2026-11-19 | The wiki eval: CI fixtures prove the runner and scorer; his 15 cases come from his own crawl, each confirmed by him (WO-8.4, plan 9, 7.8, D290-D292, checklist 3.9.6) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** (1) **Why not 15 cases now:** a case cites `w:<Title>@<revid>` revisions of his crawl, which waits on the licence check (3.9.6) and exists only on his PC; no id or page is made up. (2) **Case** (`id kind question cites facts absent confirmed`): `fact` or `injected`; the `w:` ids a right answer cites, at the revision he confirmed; the phrases it must contain (no digits: game numbers come from the DB); what it must not say (an injected claim); the day he confirmed it. His file lives at `$GOS_HOME/data/evals/wiki_questions.tsv` (**judgement call**: it is drawn from his personal cache, so GOS never commits it; he may copy it into `tests/evals/` himself). (3) **Scorer** (deterministic, reads only the cache): a case whose page left the cache or changed since he confirmed it is `not checked` and fails (re-confirm it), never passed; otherwise the answer fails when it cites a `w:` id the cache doesn't hold at that revision, misses a case page or a fact, says an `absent` phrase, states a number no cited page's text holds, or lacks the attribution line (D291). (4) **`gos eval wiki --make-cases --page TITLE ...`**: reads each page from his cache (a cached redirect followed), proposes a question, fills the `w:` id, shows the first lines and asks for the facts, the absent phrases and a yes; each kept case is written at once with the next free `wNN` id. (5) **CI**: three fixture cases (`tests/unit/wiki/fixture_cases.tsv`) over the wiki tests' own made-up pages, the injected Steam Turbine page among them, run by `--fake`, whose answerer calls the researcher's `wiki_search`/`wiki_fetch` through the registry and adds the attribution line as the deep lane does: it proves the format, the store, the tools and the scorer, not a model or the deep lane's researcher hand-off (the paid run does). (6) **Paid run**: each case a deep-lane task (`llm_usage` task `eval:wiki`) capped at `[budget] task_max_usd` (D253's per-task cap, 2.0 until `gos eval design-cost` measures it; no wiki-specific cap: an unmeasured lower one would make the result depend on the cap, not on the answers), so 15 cases cost at most 15 x that cap ($30 at 2.0), printed first. A task that reaches the cap is stopped, never approved (D157), and its case fails as `capped` (counted apart: re-run it), not as a wrong answer. The per-case cost of a wiki answer is **not measured** yet. The same refusals as D362 (5), the day-cap refusal included (the same refinement of D334), and a `yes`; it stops at the estimate. (7) **Case checks**: a tab, a line break or a `"` is refused in any cell (the file is plain TSV) with a reason, never a crash; an `injected` case needs at least one `absent` phrase; `--make-cases` flags a fact not in the page's text as typed and replaces the file atomically | — | WO-8.4 PR |
| D356 | 2026-11-19 | How GTNH 2.9.0 writes every file GOS reads in a live instance, and what a refused write costs (WO-6.8; G11 finding 1, risk 1; V27) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** Facts, each from source read at the version the 2.9.0 manifest pins (`DreamAssemblerXXL` `releases/manifests/2.9.0.json`: BetterQuesting 3.8.92-GTNH, Hodgepodge 2.7.221, VisualProspecting 1.5.42, ServerUtilities 2.4.22; Minecraft 1.7.10 as decompiled by RFG for Forge 10.13.4.1614; OpenJDK jdk8u, jdk17u, jdk21u, jdk25u master). Read to learn behaviour only; nothing copied. **(1) BetterQuesting** (`QuestProgress\<uuid>.json`, `NameCache.json`, `QuestingParties.json`, `LifeDatabase.json`, `QuestDatabase.json`): `JsonHelper.WriteToFile2(File, IOConsumer)` (JsonHelper.java:267-317 at tag 3.8.92-GTNH, commit 17d5f92) deletes and recreates `<file>.tmp`, writes it through a `FileOutputStream`, then `Files.move(tmp, file, REPLACE_EXISTING, ATOMIC_MOVE)` (:299-303); only `AtomicMoveNotSupportedException` falls back to a plain `REPLACE_EXISTING` move (:304-310); any other failure (an `AccessDeniedException`) is logged at ERROR on the `betterquesting` logger as `An error occurred while saving JSON to file (Temp copy):` (:312-313) and **not retried**. It runs on `BQThreadedIO.DISK_IO`, a 4-thread pool (BQThreadedIO.java:11-18, threads `pool-N-thread-M`), started by `SaveLoadHandler.saveDatabases()` (SaveLoadHandler.java:121-149) on every overworld `WorldEvent.Save` (EventHandler.java:395-400: each autosave, every 900 ticks with Hodgepodge's `autoSaveInterval`, D258; the pause save; quit); the server thread waits for every write (`future.get()`, :138-146). Progress files are written only for players marked dirty, and `dirtyPlayers` is cleared before the writes run (:317-321), so a refused progress write is lost until that player's progress changes again (if the game then closes, the completion is missing on disk). The non-atomic `WriteToFile(File, JsonObject)` (:208-265: `Files.move(REPLACE_EXISTING)` at :258) is used only by admin commands and loot saves (QuestCommandDefaults, LootSaveLoad, BQS_Commands), never for the files GOS reads. **(2) Hodgepodge** `level.dat` (its threaded saver is on by default, `TweaksConfig.threadedWorldDataSaving` :294-298; mixin `MixinSaveHandler_threadedIO` replaces vanilla's `saveWorldInfoWithPlayer`): `WorldDataSaver.writeData` (WorldDataSaver.java:158-238 at tag 2.7.221, commit 3a6e21e) writes `.level.dat-<n>.tmp`, forces it, copies the current `level.dat` to a temp and moves it onto `level.dat_old`, then `Files.move(temp, level.dat, ATOMIC_MOVE, REPLACE_EXISTING)` (:232, :234), on Minecraft's `ThreadedFileIOBase` thread. A failure is logged `Failed to write data to file {}` (logger `HodgepodgeWorldDataSaver`, :86) and **retried** at the next save (`failedData`, :83-85, :94-98); at world close a write still failing is logged `Closing world with unsaved data; these writes will not be replayed in another session` (:140-142). **(3) Minecraft 1.7.10** (`SaveHandler.java`): vanilla `level.dat` (only without Hodgepodge's saver): `level.dat_new` through a `FileOutputStream`, `level.dat_old` deleted, `level.dat.renameTo(level.dat_old)`, `level.dat_new.renameTo(level.dat)` (:195-216); `playerdata\<uuid>.dat`: `<uuid>.dat.tmp`, then `delete()` the old and `renameTo` (:277-286), failure logged `Failed to save player data for <name>` (:291), not retried until the next save (GOS reads playerdata only for a world without `Data.Player`, i.e. never in single-player); `stats\<uuid>.json`: commons-io `writeStringToFile`, in place (`StatisticsFile.java:69`, `Couldn't save stats` :73); region files: `RegionFile`'s `RandomAccessFile("rw")`, held open while the region is cached and written in place (not established by line: `RegionFile.java` was not re-read for this row; D260 already reads regions through `safe_open` ranges). **(4) VisualProspecting** caches (`DIM<n>.dat`): `Utils.writeNBT` (Utils.java:148-156 at 1.5.42, commit b85c353): a `FileOutputStream` on the file itself, in place, on `ThreadedFileIOBase`; failure `Failed to write NBT to file: {}`. **(5) ServerUtilities** `logs\world.log`: appended through `FileWriter(file, true)` on `ThreadedFileIOBase` at each save (ServerUtilitiesUniverseData.java:146-163 at tag 2.4.22), failure only `printStackTrace`. **(6) logs** (`latest.log`, `fml-client-latest.log`): log4j holds them open to append for the session and renames them away at start (research B section 6.2; not re-read here). **(7) GregtecLink** `gregteclink\events\machines.jsonl`: appended with `Files.write(CREATE, APPEND)` per event (EventFile.java:73-81, this repo), rotated at game start with `Files.move(REPLACE_EXISTING)` onto `machines.1.jsonl` (:43-45). **(8) OC mailbox** `outbox.json`: OC renames `outbox.tmp` to it only while it does not exist (D216-D218), so never a replace onto a file GOS reads. **(9) JourneyMap** waypoints, **crash reports**, **Prism's** `instance.cfg`/`mmc-pack.json`, **mods** (`gos doctor`, `playhooks`, `sync`): JourneyMap's writer is not established (closed source at this version); crash reports are new files; Prism and mod files are not written while the game runs. **Java on Windows** (OpenJDK `sun/nio/fs/WindowsFileCopy.move`, identical in 8, 17, 21 and 25: jdk8u :291-299, :356-389; jdk21u :318-326, :383-416; jdk25u :300-308, :365-402): `ATOMIC_MOVE` is `MoveFileExW(src, dst, MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING)`; non-atomic `REPLACE_EXISTING` is `DeleteFileW(dst)` then `MoveFileExW(src, dst, 0)`; `File.renameTo` is the C runtime's `_wrename` (WinNTFileSystem_md.c `rename0`, jdk8u :787-796, jdk21u :829-836); `FileOutputStream` opens with `FILE_SHARE_READ \| FILE_SHARE_WRITE` (io_util_md.c jdk8u :234, jdk21u :221). No JDK version uses `FileRenameInfoEx` POSIX semantics, so lwjgl3ify's Java 17-25 and Java 8 behave the same. **What Windows does with each** (measured on Windows CI by `tests/unit/test_save_collision.py`, run 44516182476, job 135532648226, with a reader holding the target 1 s): with `safe_open` as it was (read, write and delete sharing, no oplock) `MoveFileExW(REPLACE_EXISTING)` (with or without `WRITE_THROUGH`, and `os.replace`) is **refused with `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` (5)**: that is BetterQuesting's and Hodgepodge's every save, the G11 finding; `DeleteFileW` then `MoveFileExW(0)` (Java's non-atomic replace, vanilla's player `.dat`) succeeds (the runner's Windows deletes with POSIX semantics, so the name is free at once), and so do the rename away (vanilla level.dat), `ReplaceFileW`, a `FileRenameInfoEx` POSIX rename, an in-place `CREATE_ALWAYS` write and an append; a plain `open()` (no delete sharing) refuses every rename and delete (32, or 5 for the replace) and allows in-place writes and appends. **So the real game can hit it:** BetterQuesting's progress and Hodgepodge's level.dat are replaced at every save, and only a GOS read that is open at that moment refuses it; the read is milliseconds and starts only after the file held still 1 s (D257), which is why only a loaded CI runner showed it, but a refused BetterQuesting progress save is lost (above). Whether Clayton's own Windows also deletes with POSIX semantics is not established (it decides only the delete-then-move patterns, which no file GOS reads in single-player uses) | Facts for D357's design | WO-6.8 PR |
| D357 | 2026-11-19 | `safe_open` gives a file up to the game: a read-handle oplock (WO-6.8; refines D257's reads, plan 5.5, PM13) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** **Design:** on Windows every `safe_open` handle (read, sharing read, write and delete, asynchronous) is opened with `NtCreateFile` and `FILE_OPEN_REQUIRING_OPLOCK` and at once requests an oplock with `FSCTL_REQUEST_OPLOCK` at level read + handle caching (`gamestate/_win32.py`). The flag makes the open and the oplock one step (review M1): Microsoft's `NtCreateFile` page says it 'eliminates the time between when you open the file and request an oplock', and that until the request 'all subsequent attempts to open the file will be blocked'; measured on Windows CI (`test_the_open_and_the_oplock_are_one_step`, a reader held 0.3 s between its open and its request; run 44545311387, job 135634178974): with `CreateFileW` then the request, a `MoveFileExW(REPLACE_EXISTING)` in that moment is refused (`WinError 5`) for all three replace calls; with the flag every replace, `DeleteFileW`+move and rename-away made in that moment waits, then breaks the oplock and succeeds, and an in-place write (an open that shares with ours) goes on at once and the oplock then covers the new bytes. Where the volume refuses the flag (`STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER`, `NOT_SUPPORTED`, `INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST`, Windows 7's `OPLOCK_NOT_GRANTED`) or the oplock, the handle is a plain `CreateFileW` one as before, and `safe_open.report` logs a WARNING naming the file once per file and process (`no oplock on this file`, or `the oplock came after the open`); a granted one is one INFO line per process (`Windows grants read-handle oplocks with the open`), which 3.10.3's Lab check looks for (review M2). `STATUS_CANNOT_BREAK_OPLOCK` (another program's oplock our open would break) is an `OSError` the readers retry. Windows grants it on a quiet file. A replace onto the file, a rename or delete of it, or an open that would conflict breaks the handle part and **makes the game's call wait** for our handle to close instead of refusing it; an open to write it (an in-place save) breaks the read part. A thread per open handle waits for the break and closes the handle at once, under the lock every chunk read (`CHUNK`, 1 MiB) takes, so never in the middle of a read and never later than one chunk read, however slow the caller is (`LeasedFile` in `safe_open.py`). The read is then marked `interrupted`: further reads raise `ReadInterrupted` (an `OSError`), `read_bytes`, `read_range`, `sha256_file` and `copy_to` check it before they return, and the usual 3 tries with backoff read the file again. **Evidence** (`tests/unit/test_save_collision.py`, `serial`): every pattern of D356's table against a reader held 1 s: under the oplock **no pattern is refused**, the target ends with the new content and the held reader is told every time (its read raises `ReadInterrupted`, in-place writes and appends included: review L3, run 44545311387), and the writer's call waited at most 7 ms (run 44519813224, job 135545243580: 0.001 s for `MoveFileExW(REPLACE_EXISTING)`, 0.007 s with `WRITE_THROUGH`; the test bounds it at 0.5 s); on the commit before (`4ce23c7`, run 44516182476) the same cells for the old reader were refused (D356). The soak (`test_soak.py`) now holds the first read of `QuestProgress\<uuid>.json` and of level.dat until the game has saved that file (two save cycles in its log, or the oplock's break): on `4ce23c7` it failed on Windows deterministically with BetterQuesting's `WinError 5` (run 44516182476, job 135532648226); with the fix every forced hold ends in the game's break and no save is refused. Linux runs the same logic on a fake handle (`tests/unit/test_safe_open_lease.py`: chunked reads, a break mid-read closes between chunks, an idle reader still gives way, a break after the last chunk drops the read, retries, give-up after 3, `read_range` keeping a range read before a break and dropping a handle closed before its chunk, and `report`'s log lines). **Measured on CI, not assumed:** the oplock is granted even beside a handle that is open to write (log4j's log), and an append through that handle breaks it (run 44519813224). So `read_range`, which reads logs, `machines.jsonl` and region headers (appended or patched in place, never replaced), keeps a range it read when a break came during it (its callers read a range measured by `stat` first and check it, `validate` retries, D260's chunk checks, as before); only a handle closed before a chunk drops it. The whole-file readers (`read_bytes`, `sha256_file`, `copy_to`) drop any read a break touched. **One place:** `files.read_range` (logs, census) is now `safe_open.read_range`; `ops.worldbackup.copy_in` uses `safe_open.copy_to`; every other reader already called `read_bytes`/`read_text`/`sha256_file` (a sweep of `core/gregtec` for `open(`, `read_bytes(` and `read_text(` on game paths found no other reader of a live instance). **Rejected:** (a) open, read and close within a measured quiet window (D257 as it was): shortens the window, never closes it, which is the G11 finding; (b) a private copy first (`CopyFileW`, `BackupRead`): both open the source the same way, so they collide the same way; (c) a hard-link snapshot: the link is the same file, so a handle through it blocks the replace the same way, and it needs write access to the game folder; (d) asking the game to save differently (POSIX rename semantics): a mod change in Play for a reader's problem, and lwjgl3ify's JDKs don't offer it (D356); (e) not reading BetterQuesting files while the game runs: loses `quest_done`. **The CLAUDE.md rule, mapped:** "share-delete" stays (the oplock is on top of it); "copy then parse" stays (read whole into memory, close, then parse); "retry x3" stays (an interrupted read is one of the retried errors); "never hold handles" is now enforced by Windows itself: a handle is closed the moment the game wants the file, and always at the end of the read. **Licences:** the Win32 calls are re-implemented from Microsoft's documentation; nothing vendored | Refines D257 (reads) and plan 5.5; answers G11 finding 1 | WO-6.8 PR |
| D358 | 2026-11-19 | What WO-6.8 leaves for Clayton's PC, and the fake game (WO-6.8; checklist 3.10.3, 3.15.4, 5.2.11; V27) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** (1) **PC-only:** CI proves the mechanism on Windows Server's NTFS; Clayton's Windows 11, his antivirus (which also opens files the game writes) and the real game with lwjgl3ify's JDK are checked only in Lab: checklist 3.10.3's 2-hour session, then the runbook's (`watchers.md` §4.1) `Select-String` for every mod's save-failure line, quoted from their source (D356). Pass: no lines. V27 stays open until then. (2) **Play's watchers** (3.15.4) wait for that Lab session to pass, as before, but no longer for a design decision: 5.2.11's default (the follow-up WO) is done. (3) **The fake game** already models the real patterns (BetterQuesting and Hodgepodge: temp file then `os.replace` = `MoveFileExW(REPLACE_EXISTING)`; vanilla level.dat by renames; stats and VisualProspecting in place; logs appended through held handles), so it is unchanged; the soak gained the forced long read. (4) **Judgement calls:** (a) an interrupted read is dropped even when the game only replaced the file (the old bytes were whole) — simpler and never wrong, at the cost of one more read; (b) the oplock is requested for every file, logs included, rather than a list of replaced files, so a file GOS doesn't know the game replaces is covered too; (c) tests that asserted the old behaviour were changed to the new contract, not loosened: `test_safe_open.py::test_replace_onto_the_held_file_is_refused` became `..._goes_on_and_the_read_is_dropped`, and the soak's Windows control reads before the rename it makes in the same thread; (d) a GOS process stopped in a debugger or suspended while holding a file would make the game's save wait, and that wait is bounded only by how soon GOS's process runs the thread that closes the handle (it needs the GIL); a killed GOS closes its handles. The wait reaches the game's tick: BetterQuesting saves on `WorldEvent.Save` (`EventHandler.java` 396-398, tag 3.8.92-GTNH), and `SaveLoadHandler.saveDatabases` waits for each write with `future.get()` (`SaveLoadHandler.java` 138-140) on the server thread, so a stalled GOS stalls the game's save (and its tick) instead of losing the write. Windows CI measured the wait at 8 ms at most (run 44532344535); (e) a read that goes without the oplock (not granted, or taken after the open) is logged, not refused: watchers on such a volume still work, the WARNING names the file, and 3.10.3's oplock check makes it a failed Lab check (review M2) | Leaves V27 for the Lab session | WO-6.8 PR |
| D364 | 2026-11-19 | Recipes that give something from no input they use up: the cause, and the export's `unkeyed` marker (follow-up to WO-4.2; plan 5.2 step 2a, 0.1 principles 1 and 3; D233, D234) | **Pending Clayton (PR questions).** **Finding:** on the server harness DB, 88 GT recipes (90 of 99,692 `gt_recipes.jsonl` lines; two pairs are copies) have an output and no input with an amount above 0, in 12 maps. The export dropped no input of theirs: every item and fluid it names is registered (`counts.unregistered` 0, the harness's `item_refs_registered`). GT's own recipes are like that in the dev runtime, for three reasons, traced in GT 5.09.54.265's source. **(1) By design, 66 lines:** GT++'s fishing pond (3), algae pond (16), rock breaker (2) and matter fabricator (2) are registered with a programmed circuit or nothing, and the machine works from EU and its own code; Eye of Harmony (43) keeps its hydrogen and helium in private fields of the recipe's special object. **(2) An item the dev runtime lacks, 11 lines:** a recipe loader asks for an item of a mod that isn't loaded, or an oredict name with no member, and gets null. GT's recipe builder drops trailing nulls (`ArrayExt.removeTrailingNulls`) and keeps the others. Dropped: GT's centrifuge recipes for Twilight Forest's meats become methane from circuit 1 alone (4 lines; GT++ copies them to `gt.recipe.multicentrifuge`, 2 lines, `r:gt.recipe.multicentrifuge/5a3d6c4c649f` among them), and the assembler's ender pearl from Forestry's item becomes circuit 5 alone (1). Kept as a null slot: 2 macerator recipes (`cropChilipepper` has no member) and 2 extruder recipes; WO-1.3 leaves null slots out, as the contract's positional arrays ask. **(3) Fake display recipes, 13 lines:** the tree farm (12) and the mass fabricator (1). So no registry name is missing and there is nothing the export could key; the full pack has those mods, so group 2 should be empty there (to be read off the first real Export run). **Decision:** the mod marks each such line at the source with `"unkeyed"` and what it can see of the cause: `null_input` (a null input slot), `special_items` (no null slot, the line has special items) or `none` (a circuit or nothing: groups 1 and 2 look the same once GT has built the recipe). Every line with an output and no used-up input has it and no other line does; `counts.recipes_unkeyed` counts them; no numeric ID is involved. `gos db build` keeps it as `recipe_meta` `unkeyed`, outside the content hash (a run made before D364 gives the same keys and hashes) and outside `recipe_conditions` (it is not a machine condition). Both harnesses check it (`recipes_without_used_up_input_marked`), and `tests/integration/test_db_harness.py` runs the SQL over the harness DB: the rows found are exactly the marked rows. **Judgement call:** the marker is derived in the mod, an exception to the contract's "the mod dumps raw facts, Python normalises", because the request was for the export run itself to say which recipes these are. **Unchanged:** D233's and D234's guards (a power line leaves such a recipe out with a note; `validate` refuses it). They read the DB's inputs, so they also hold for runs made before D364, and `none` cannot tell a fishing pond from a dropped input. Their comments now give this cause instead of "an input the export couldn't key". **Open (PR questions):** (a) 396 more lines have a null input slot beside an input that is used up, so the DB has them with the inputs GT has: 278 are the primitive blast furnace's empty second slot (by design), 118 in 14 other maps are not traced one by one (the brewer's Leninade without `cropLemon` is one) and are not marked, because the line alone doesn't tell an empty slot from a missing item; (b) Eye of Harmony's hydrogen and helium could be exported from the recipe object's getters; (c) whether the designers may count on a `none` recipe of a machine known to work from EU alone | — (D233 and D234 stand; their stated cause, "an input it couldn't key", is corrected) | Clayton, 2026-11-18 (export fix after WO-4.2); GT 5.09.54.265 source; server harness run |
| D341 | 2026-11-18 | Recipe cards: `fake?` rows after the recipes whose status is known | `recipe_find` and `recipe_uses` list a recipe whose fake status is unknown (`fake IS NULL`: an NEI-only handler that doesn't say, D105, flagged `fake?`) after the recipes whose status is known, in the non-recycling group and again in the recycling one. `recipes_for_item.sql`: `ORDER BY recycling, r.fake IS NULL, r.voltage_tier IS NOT NULL, r.voltage_tier, machine, r.ref`; `recipes_using_item.sql`: `ORDER BY recycling, r.fake IS NULL, machine, r.voltage_tier IS NOT NULL, r.voltage_tier, r.ref`. The rest of D132's order stays (recycling last; `recipe_find`: no tier before the lowest tier, machine, key; `recipe_uses`: machine before tier). **Why:** an NEI-only handler's rows have no voltage tier (all but the 725 of `gt.recipe.fog_molten`), so D132 listed them with crafting and smelting, by machine name, and often first, while plan 7.3 wants the simplest recipe at his tier and nothing says such a row is a recipe at all (the space pumps, the gas siphon and "Underground Fluid Stats" are NEI information pages). **Measured** on a DB built from the client harness (dev runtime, run `20261118T015304Z`; 3,909 `fake?` recipes from 30 handlers among the 141,395 the tools show by default): of the 42,852 items and fluids a recipe makes, 2,040 have a `fake?` row; for 380 it was the first row though a known recipe that isn't recycling exists (Sulfuric Acid: Space Pumping Module, then Underground Fluid Stats; Oxygen: Planetary Gas Siphon; Steel Plate: IC2's Metal Former; Steel Gear: TConstruct's Casting Table), now for none; 1,422 have only `fake?` rows and keep their order. `recipe_uses`, by shown input slot: 346 of 25,064 items and fluids, now none. **`show_fake`:** rows flagged `fake` or `disabled` have a known status and keep their place by tier; they are listed only on request (judgement call: the smallest change; ranking them is not asked for). **What follows:** a result's first row is its first row without a `fake?` or `recycling` flag whenever it has one, on every page and under every filter. So the dashboard's recipe card, which keeps the result's order (D160), starts with a known recipe; the instant template (`ask.card_text`) names the card's first row again ("The first is ..."; "the first unflagged one" is gone), and "What's this?" (`gamelink.whatsthis`, WO-8.1) the same: both copies of D172's skip-the-flagged-rows rule are removed. The recipe-lookup skill keeps "the first row without a flag" (a model still picks among rows, and with `show_fake` a `fake` row can stand above a plain one); its line on `recipe_find`'s order and "even when a flagged row is listed above it" are reworded. The eval is unchanged: D171's truth and D172's oracles still leave flagged rows out. On that DB `gos eval fast-lane --fake` gives 60 of 60 and `gos eval recipe-lookup --fake` 50 of 50 with 0 uncited numbers, before and after; the only changed text of the 50 is r06's instant reply ("The first is Shaped Crafting"). **Not in this change (each needs its own decision row):** rows whose status is known but that aren't a way to make the item. (1) NEI furnace recipes that melt a GT part back into its material ("Smelting: Long Bronze Rod" for a bronze ingot; PR #54 Q3, not yet answered): 3,697 of the dev runtime's 5,796 furnace recipes take an item with a GT part prefix (not dust, ore, raw ore, crushed or gem) and give the ingot or nugget of the same material, 864 take a dust, ore or gem of it, and 61 take an item with no GT prefix (machines, tools, cans; some of them real recipes, such as IC2's uranium), which a same-material rule would miss; a rule also has to say where `recycling` is computed (the three card queries, the eval's own SQL and the planner's graph each derive it from the category today) and changes the eval's "simplest" rows. (2) A canner emptying a cell is now the first row for Sulfuric Acid and Oxygen (ULV, the lowest tier; D328 calls decanting importing in the power designer) | Refines D132 (recipe cards: where `fake?` rows sort), D170 (the skill's wording on the order), D172 (the instant template's first row; "the card's order stays D132's") | Clayton's task (G2 review, PR #54 Q2); this PR |
| D364 | 2026-11-20 | The core makes CPython's full garbage collections 100 times rarer (QA 10-13; WO-3.9, D353-D354, checklist 7.3) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** **Why:** a design runs on a thread inside the core process, and every full (gen-2) collection of its millions of planner objects holds the GIL, so the event loop that answers the dashboard, voice and OC stalled: on the client harness DB a cold power design (Best, LV) had 19-24 full collections, up to 0.29-0.37 s each, the loop's lag up to 0.36 s. **Design:** `gregtec.api.server.run` calls `tune_gc()` once it holds its port: `gc.collect()`, then `gc.set_threshold(700, 10, 1000)` (gen-0 and gen-1 at CPython's defaults, full collections about 100 times rarer). Measured the same way: no full collection in the cold or the warm design, the loop's lag at most 16-24 ms, the design results byte-identical, peak RSS no higher (315-321 MB against 326-332 MB). `gc.freeze()` alone only halved the cost and is not used. **Watch:** cyclic garbage is collected less often; check the core's memory (Task Manager) over the Lab sitting and the fake day one: steady, not climbing. **Test:** `tests/unit/test_core_gc.py`. | - | QA 10-13 (`qa/w10-w13-fixes`) |
| D366 | 2026-11-20 | Tools read only the structure rows they need; build_request's connection allows 4 MB values (refines D130's 1 MB limit, D312) | **Why:** on a real `gos structures extract` dump (297 controllers, 455 variants) every `build_request` failed with "database error: string or blob too big": the layout planner read every structure row (`footprints.library`), and Forge of the Gods' t3 row holds 1,239,746 bytes of `blocks_json`, over D130's 1 MB value limit on a tool's connection (SQLite refuses to read such a value, not only to make one). CI and the G10 review had only stand-in structures. **Design:** `footprints.library(conn, mtes)` reads only the named GT machines' controllers (and rows linked to them by `machine_id`), every row of each; the layout passes its plans' machines, so a shell, a layout and their tests never read another multi's cells. The guard stays: every tool keeps the 1 MB limit except `build_request` (`Tool.max_value_bytes`, `shells.MAX_STRUCTURE_BYTES` = 4 MB), whose layout reads its plans' own structure rows whole, which for Forge of the Gods is over 1 MB. The CLI, the dashboard's guide and the API read without the tools' guard, as before. **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_shell.py` (a row over 1 MB beside the plans' rows, and a plan of that multi), `tests/integration/test_real_dump_harness.py` on CI's real dump (the `mod` job now runs `gos structures extract`). | Refines D130 (tool value limit) and D312 (the layout reads footprints) | `fix/build-request-real-dump` |

| D367 | 2026-11-20 | Shell designs and their versions: the chain, undo, cost, and what a revision reads (WO-10.10 (A), plan 7.10 "Shell designer", D314, D316-D318) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** `gregtec.build.shell_versions`. **Design** = a chain of saved builds around one set of plans: `GOS_HOME/data/build/designs/<ds-id>.json` (`ds-` + 12 hex of its first build; a build made before WO-10.10 is listed as that one-version design and gets its file the first time it is revised or made current, never on a read) holds every version's build id, `parent` (the version it was revised from), `asked` (at most 200 characters: Clayton's own request when a lane passes it on, else the words given), `by` (`procedural` = GOS's generator or reviser; `architect` = the model through `build_revise`), time, `cost_usd`, status and blocks; `current`; and `history`, every change of current (designed, revised, made current, undo), at most 1000 kept. `current.json` names the design he works on (else the one touched last). Builds stay WO-10.7's immutable folders (the chain lives only in the design file, so a build shared by two designs is never rewritten). **Nothing is ever deleted** (as D314's theme versions): undo = the current version's parent made current again; any version can be made current; at most 200 versions a design (then start a new one; none is dropped to make room). **Revise** (`revise`): the current version's own files (`design.json`, `layout.json`) plus the block catalog for the palette rule, never the plans, footprints or `structure` rows (the real dump's rows are up to ~1.2 MB and the tools' read guard stops such a read; the layout the version was designed against is the one revised; `gos shell export` still re-lays the plans out and refuses a changed layout, D317); new operations from the model or GOS's reviser (D368); `max_blocks`, `headroom` and another theme's palette (its rules and swatches; a prompt-only build's extra swatches are then dropped) on top; then the **same gate** (`shell.design`). **Refused, nothing written, the current version stays**: the reviser can't act on the words, the gate refuses, the result is a version the design already has ("make it current instead"; the current one itself: "nothing changes"), or the design moved on while it ran. **Cost** (judgement call): GOS's own is `0.0`; the architect's is recorded as none and shown "AI (see Costs)", because a tool call can't see its deep-lane task's running cost; the task's cost is on the Costs view under its task. *Review* (advisor: store the task id in the version so the Costs row can be linked): **not done yet**: the tool can't see its task either (the in-process MCP server's handler runs on the warm client, outside the task, and a tool `Call` carries no task id); giving it one goes through `agents/tools/registry.py`, which another lane is changing (the read guard), so it is a follow-up; until then the version's `at` and `asked` and the Costs view's task (`deep:<id>`, its request) match by hand. **Tool** `build_revise(words, design?, design_ops?, theme?, max_blocks?, headroom?, save?)`: deep lane only, the architect's and the orchestrator's, data only (no code, no world action, no `.schematic`); `save` false shows the current operations and GOS's reviser's attempt and writes nothing. `build_request`, `gos shell design` and the Designer's button start a design. `gos shell versions|revise|undo|current|compare`. | D316 (builds now also belong to designs) | WO-10.10 PR; plan 7.10 |
| D368 | 2026-11-20 | GOS's own reviser, the roof's pitch, and the revise eval (WO-10.10 (A), D316, D317) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** **Reviser** (`gregtec.build.shell_revise`, pure; the No-AI path and the test baseline for revisions, as the procedural generator is for designs): the words split into requests ("taller, and no windows on the north wall" is two; a list of sides stays one), each applied to the saved operations, the rest copied unchanged: the roof (steeper/pitched/gable: flat becomes a gable, a gable's pitch +1 to 3; flatter; flat; parapet or none), the height (taller/shorter, 2 blocks or `by N`: the walls' top course, the eaves band on it, pillars, the roof and the lights move together and the headroom with them; never lower than the layout's top, which the walls must close), windows (none, none on a side, add, on a side, more/fewer, bigger/smaller, taller/shorter), lights and pillars (none, add, more, fewer), the plinth and the eaves band. **A request it can't act on in full is refused whole** (judgement call: never half of what he asked, silently), as is one that changes nothing ("as far as that goes already"). The headroom is raised so the roof fits (at most 16; a roof that still doesn't fit is closed flat at the top, as before), never lowered. **Vocabulary**: `roof` gains `pitch` 1-3 (blocks a gable's slope rises a step); 1 is exactly the cells of before (tested), so no saved build changes. **Eval** `gos eval shell-revise PLAN... [--fake]`, cases `tests/evals/shell_revise.tsv` (6 briefs with a follow-up each; no game number or id; *review*: `windows.sides-north`, the old sides less north and every other side kept, and a no-north-windows case that touches windows only, so `kept` checks the rest): each brief designed procedurally and started, the follow-up given to the reviser under test, scored by the gate (a new version), `asked` (the case's checks on the new operations against the old) and `kept` (every op kind the case doesn't touch unchanged); `--fake`/CI: GOS's reviser; paid (his PC): the architect through the deep lane as the router hands it, one task each, capped by `[budget] task_max_usd`, estimate $0.50-$1.00 a case (from D318's shell estimate; not measured). Both on a scratch copy of the DB, vault and plans (`data/eval/shell-revise-home`). Scorer mutations tested (a reviser that also drops the plinth fails `kept`, one that changes the wrong part fails `asked`, one that also drops another side's windows fails `asked`, one that writes nothing fails the gate). *Review*: the headroom note ("headroom 16 (the most) ...") is given only when a revision changes the headroom. | - | WO-10.10 PR; checklist 3.13.9 |
| D369 | 2026-11-20 | Revise or new, and the lane, decided by the router in code (WO-10.10 (A), plan 4.4, D150, D318) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** `router.route(text, shell=...)`: `shell` is whether a design exists (the asker reads `current.json`, else the newest design). With one: "undo", "undo that", "go back to the previous version", "revert", "put it back" are the instant `shell_undo` (code makes the parent current, no model); words whose change word has a building part as its object within three words ("no lights", "add more lights", "swap the windows for reinforced glass"), a part or the whole shell/building followed by a comparative ("make the roof steeper", "make the building taller") or a bare comparative ("taller, and no windows on the north wall", "a bit steeper") are `shell_revise`; never a question ("which roof is steeper"), never "new/another/fresh/different ... shell/building/design", "from scratch" or "start over", never a creation verb with something to create ("design a shell around my EBF plan with no windows", "make a shell for plan p-1 with a steeper roof", "make a gothic theme with no lights": those reach WO-10.7's design rule, `build_request`, or the theme rule, `theme_save`); whenever WO-10.7's design rule (design|make|build|draw|wrap|put ... shell|building|facade: "wrap the EBF in a shell with no windows", "build the ebf a shell with a steeper roof", "make the EBF a building") or a WO-10.6 theme rule matches, except the shell itself made more ("make the shell taller"); never "a/an ... shell" ("a shell around the cleanroom ...") or "a/an ... part" ("build a taller wall", "add a wall of solar panels"), which is a new thing, never words naming a plan or another part of the base ("remove the walls in plan x", "add more lights to my base", "add more lamps to the nether portal": a place after a preposition with no word for the shell or a place on it), and a bare comparative followed by another noun ("lower the tier") is not one; "building" or "shell" alone is never a part ("I need more power for the building" is the fast lane's). *Review* (advisor: keep "no time window", exclude creation and narrow the parts): done; *final check* (creation phrasings without "a/an" still revised): every creation rule now wins; the router's test has each of the reviewer's probes. Known limit: words with no part ("use brick instead of stone") are not a revision in Chat; the workbench's Ask the architect names its design, so there they are (D370). Without a design nothing changes. **Lanes** (judgement call): `shell_revise` goes to the deep lane's architect with the design named in the task (`shell_versions.revise_task`: "revise ... with build_revise ...; never start a new shell", Clayton's words the only instruction); with the AI off or no key, GOS's own reviser revises it there and then in code (lane `no_ai`, the shell card in the reply), so voice and Chat still iterate without AI. The fast lane never revises (its tools can't write). | D318 (router rule for shells, kept) | WO-10.10 PR; plan 4.4 |
| D370 | 2026-11-20 | The workbench in the Build view: jobs, sizes, the 3D viewer and its ghosts, compare (WO-10.10 (B)-(D), plan 7.14, D92, D304, D319, D353) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton (board approval).** **Jobs**: WO-3.9's `DesignJobs` gains the kind `shell` (`POST /api/shells/jobs` `{design}` or `{revise}`, admin or paired device; stages layout, palette, compose, gate, saving, each a cancel point before anything is written; one shell job at a time; it shares the planner's lock, so it waits behind a running design, cancellable). The panel says the free Design new uses the theme, plans and sizes and keeps the words only as the design's request (*review*). **Sizes**: only `max_blocks` and `headroom`, the gate's own inputs; the layout's clearances are not offered (judgement call: `gos shell export` lays the plans out again with the defaults and refuses a different layout, D317, so a shell designed with another clearance could never be exported). **Viewer**: the build guide's own drawing (`Scene`, extracted from WO-10.8's viewer, D319: turn, tilt, zoom, compass, hover/tap, layer slider, that layer's materials) fed by `GET /api/shells/{id}/view`: the build's cells recomputed from its own files with no DB (its operations against its saved layout less the volumes the gate cleared, refused if the count differs), names and face colours from the catalog when there is one; at most 60 000 cells drawn; the floor plan's machines (`mte:` cited) and keep-outs as dashed outlines inside it, toggleable. The e2e runs the workbench on a fourth test core (`serve_core.py --shells`: WO-10.7's test home and one shell). Built to the design system; no board yet. | - | WO-10.10 PR; checklist 5.10.15 |
| D371 | 2026-11-20 | CI's `checks` job may run 45 minutes, not 30 | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** The `checks` job's limit (30 minutes since PR #76, set when a Windows run took about 10) cancelled a green run: on PR #143's head `7fbd56f` the Windows job's parallel step passed (11,365 passed in 1,762 s) and the job was cancelled 17 s into the serial step. The suite has grown to about 11,400 tests; the same job took 17 to 24 minutes on `main` that day. The limit is now 45 minutes for both systems; nothing else in the job changes, no test is skipped or moved, and a hung test still cannot hold a runner for hours. If Windows runs keep climbing past about 30 minutes, the fix is to cut the time (more of D340's kind of work), not to raise the limit again. | - | PR #143 (WO-10.10), run on `7fbd56f`; D340; checklist 5.12.9 |
| D372 | 2026-11-20 | Tools without the DB page like the others; a writing tool answers in one page; effort reads only the structure variants that fit (refines plan 5.3's paging, D138's `needs_db=False`, D366) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** **Why:** two agents' `effort_estimate` on real steel plans built from a harness export failed with "has no DB to page from" once the effort table ran past one page (50 rows; the client harness DB's three MV steel routes have 54, 59 and 173 rows): the registry refused a second page of any tool registered with `needs_db=False`, so `plan_get` (nodes, alternatives, routes), `plan_export`, `plan_list`, `theme_list`, `telemetry_get` and `crop_status` failed the same way past 50 rows. And `effort.structure_blocks` read every structure variant of a plan's multis, so under a tool's 1 MB value limit (D366) a plan with Forge of the Gods (t3: 1,239,746 bytes) failed with "string or blob too big" although effort counts the smallest variant (t1: 469,878 bytes). **Paging:** a tool without the DB issues a `more` cursor like any other; its epoch is the tool's own, `Result.epoch` (`tsv.digest` of every row of its table: a plan file, the plan list, the themes), not the DB's, and a cursor is refused ("what <tool> reads changed since this cursor was issued") once those rows change. Telemetry and the breeding stations' status set none (their ages change on every read): their cursors are never refused, as live readings. A tool that writes (`design_production`, `plan_pin`, `design_power`, `build_request`, `build_revise`, ...) gets every row in one page (`registry.WHOLE`): a cursor re-runs the tool, which would run its write again (on the harness, `build_request` with a page of 2 ran four times for one answer). **Before the first DB build:** on a `gos.sqlite` that only the core's Store has made (QA 5-9's state) a DB tool's result past one page (`events_recent`, `session_summary`) failed with "there is no DB ... yet", because the cursor's epoch asked for the export run; such a DB's epoch is now empty and those tools page. **Checked:** every registered tool, by making each successful tool call of the unit tests again with a page of 2 and following its cursors to the end against one whole page, and on the harness DB; no other tool failed. **Structures:** `structure_blocks` reads each variant's size first (`octet_length`, which SQLite answers without loading the value) and loads only the variants under the connection's value limit; a longer one is left out while a shorter one fits (on the real dump, 297 controllers, the variant with the fewest bytes is always the one with the fewest blocks); when none fits it reads the shortest, and the limit's error says so. The 1 MB limit stays on every tool but `build_request` (D366). **Also, wording only:** when Play's mods can't be listed (or no Play is set), `gos pack watch` says of an addon watched always "couldn't look in Play's mods", no longer "not in Play", which isn't known then. **Tests:** `tests/unit/tools/test_tool_paging.py`, `tests/unit/planner/test_effort_structure_blocks.py`, `tests/integration/test_real_dump_harness.py` (effort_estimate paged to the end on a real steel plan; the steel plan plus the dump's largest structure under the tools' guard). | Refines plan 5.3 (paging), D138 (`needs_db=False`), D366 (what a tool reads of a structure) | `fix/building-followups`; checklist 5.12.17, 5.11.15 |
| D373 | 2026-11-20 | Addon updates checked against GTNH 2.9.0: TST 0.8.2 and PlanNH 0.2.4 pinned for the Lab test; 123Technology, Programmable Hatches and worldedit-gtnh wait (plan 13.1; checklist 3.4.3, 3.15.9, 5.2.13, 7.1) | **Clayton, 2026-11-20:** "Id really like to get TST, 123Technology and PH in if they are ready". **PlanNH and worldedit-gtnh: PM decision under D158, pending Clayton** (the PM recommended them for the Lab; he did not object). **Ready** means all of: upstream does not call the release a pre-release, WIP, RC or a build for a newer pack (Programmable Hatches tags everything `-beta`, so there the author's README table decides, D88); it is built against GTNH 2.9.0's mod versions (DreamAssemblerXXL's `2.9.0.json`: GT 5.09.54.265, NEI 2.8.171-GTNH, GTNHLib 0.11.54); it needs no library that can't be pinned; no open upstream issue reports a crash or world corruption with it on 2.9.0; and, for a mod already in Play, its changes name nothing save-breaking. Checked on 2026-11-20 against each upstream repository itself (its tags, the files at the tag, the releases page, the open issues by their titles); what could not be verified counts as not ready.
