Items and blocks were not compared one by one; one source file was deleted between the tags (a maths helper). Its Bottled Chaos recipes stop the game if no fluid `mutagen` exists; 2.9.0 ships gendustry 1.9.13-GTNH, which registers it (123Technology's build file reports that crash in its own dev setup, which has no gendustry). 24 open issues, none reporting a crash or corruption on 2.9.0. `TwistSpaceTechnology-0.8.2.jar`: 29,683,612 bytes, SHA256 `5d5a537af18baea5b49efeafa200bd4c2869c4b100b6b1ba52b4e6fd80f427ed`. **(2) PlanNH 0.2.4: ready; pinned, Lab first** (PM decision under D158, pending Clayton). Tag `0.2.4` = `45a0d1e`, released 2026-11-13, marked Latest, not a pre-release (0.2.1 groups, 0.2.2 UI, 0.2.3 configs, 0.2.4 GuideNH export). Its `dependencies.gradle` and `gradle.properties` are unchanged from 0.2.0, the version in Play since D94, so it compiles against nothing newer; its one new mixin hooks `codechicken.nei.ItemZoom` (`draw`, `resize`, `lastKeyTyped`), which NEI 2.8.171-GTNH (Play, Lab) and 2.8.172-GTNH (Export) both have. Client only: nothing of it is in a world. Charts gained fields (groups, a summary filter); that a chart saved by 0.2.0 still opens is checked in the Lab. 2 open issues, neither a crash. GTNH's daily pack ships 0.2.4. `plannh-0.2.4.jar`: 14,495,171 bytes, SHA256 `671e54712deb732264ef51aed1fde6134fc6257f193938df53d84c247f10cbd7`. Both jars are the GitHub release assets (two full downloads hashed the same; never run here). **(3) 123Technology 2.2.1.3: not ready; Play keeps 2.2.1.1.** 2.2.1.3 (2026-11-15, marked Latest) says in its release notes "Contains WIP Content"; 2.2.1.2 (2026-11-13) is a pre-release whose notes say "WIP DO NOT USE". Since 2.2.1.2 the mod id is `123technology`, no longer `123Technology` (commit `a6f99d3`; old block and item names are remapped, nothing else), `@Mod` requires TST (`required-after:TwistSpaceTechnology`), and it compiles against TST's 0.8.0 dev jar because, its build file says, TST 0.8.1 crashed entering a world there. It is built with `gtnhVersion = "2.9.0"`; 7 open issues, none a crash. **It waits for** a release its author does not call work in progress; `gos pack watch` will report it. **(4) Programmable Hatches v0.2.1p9-beta: not ready; Play keeps 0.2.1p0** (D88's rule holds). The README on the default branch `290-daily-latest` (the same text at tag `v0.2.1p9-beta`, 2026-11-19) still maps GTNH "2.9.0" to 0.2.1p0 and points a separate row, "2.9.0 Daily", at the newest release; it calls the development branch one for testing, not for an important save without a backup. The notes of v0.2.1p7 say only pack 2.9.0 is supported from that version on, and p4 fixed a bug p1 introduced (#353), so the table may change; p9 still compiles against GT 5.09.54.264, as p0 does. 48 open issues; of the 25 newest, read by title, none names a crash or corruption with p9 on 2.9.0 (older crash reports #284, #262 and #258 are open), and the rest were not read; #323 stays closed. **It waits for** the author's README naming a version newer than 0.2.1p0 for GTNH 2.9.0; `gos pack watch` will report it. **(5) worldedit-gtnh 1.0.1: not ready; Play keeps v0.0.10** (PM decision under D158, pending Clayton). 1.0.0 and 1.0.1 (both 2026-11-10; 1.0.1 marked Latest) are a rewrite (557 files changed since v0.0.10): edits spread over server ticks, Brigadier commands registered through GTNHLib, a new config file. It builds against CodeChickenCore 1.5.0, ForgeMultipart 1.8.0, ArchitectureCraft 1.13.0 and CarpentersBlocks 3.8.0-GTNH, with NEI 2.9.2-GTNH in its dev runtime, where GTNH 2.9.0 ships 1.4.22, 1.7.18, 1.12.19, 3.7.3-GTNH and NEI 2.8.171-GTNH (GTNHLib 0.11.54 and ServerUtilities 2.4.22 do match): the pack line after 2.9.0. Whether it runs on 2.9.0's versions, and whether 2.9.0's GTNHExtLib 1.0.5 supplies the Brigadier library it expects in game: not established. It now requires GTNHLib (`required-after:gtnhlib`). V16's issues: #30 (meta above 15) was closed on 2026-11-10 with 1.0.0, and the README now says block ids and metadata above the vanilla limits work; #24 (EndlessIDs schematic save and load) was closed on 2026-11-20; #28 (chunk corruption on a meta that doesn't exist) is still open. Its `.schematic` reader keeps the tags GOS writes (`BlockMapping`, `AddBlocks`, `AddData`, `WEOffset`), but GregtecLink's schematic harness compiles against v0.0.10 (D310), so the shell export and paste path is proven on v0.0.10 only; V16 stays as it is. **It waits for** a worldedit-gtnh build made against GTNH 2.9.0's mod versions, or the next stable GTNH, and then a re-run of WO-10.4's round trip on it; `gos pack watch` will report it. **Order for (1) and (2):** Lab first, in one sitting with GT-Not-Leisure (D365): checklist 3.4.3, runbook `addon_lab_test.md`. Each REPLACES a jar: in Lab's `mods` the old jar is removed (two versions of one mod won't start), and temporary `[sync.lab] exclude` entries (D93 (5)) keep the sync from deleting the new jar and putting the old one back until Play has the new one. The test: load `lab-ops`, NEI opens, one TST multi forms and runs a recipe, one 123Technology multi too (2.2.1.1 finds TST's liquid mana and items by name), PlanNH opens a chart made with 0.2.0, beside D365's GTNL checks and the PH #323 re-test; on a crash the new jars come out one at a time and the crash report and `fml-client-latest.log` are sent. `TwistSpaceTechnology-0.8.0.jar` and `plannh-0.2.0.jar` stay pinned and fetched: they are Play's jars and the rollback. **Play gets a new jar only after Clayton's Lab test passes and he says yes** (5.2.13, 3.15.9), and he copies it in himself. `fetch_play_files.ps1` lists the two under "NOT into Play yet (D373)" with the jar each replaces; `data/curated/addons.tsv` keeps its patterns, which match the old and the new file name. Nothing here writes to Play, Lab or Export. | Refines D88 and D90 (Play's addon versions; they stay until the Lab test and Clayton's yes); extends D365's Lab sitting; D88's Programmable Hatches rule and V16 are unchanged; plan 13.1's rows point here | Clayton, 2026-11-20 (TST, 123Technology, PH); PM under D158 (PlanNH, worldedit-gtnh); `plan/addon-updates-lab` |
| D374 | 2026-11-20 | The shell designer's provider seam: brief in, operations out, no tools; Anthropic and OpenAI behind it (WO-10.11 (A)-(C), plan 7.10, 4.4, D155, D316, D317) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** **Why:** Clayton, 2026-11-20: "I do think a bakeoff so to speak would be a good idea so i can also test out the framework of the building generator app". **How a design reaches a model today** (read from the code first): only through the deep lane. The router hands his words to the Claude Agent SDK's orchestrator, whose `architect` subagent (`[models] architect`, 30 turns) calls the `build_request` tool with `design`, a JSON list of `shell.OPS`; `shell_store.make` runs the gate (D317) and a refusal goes back as the tool's error ("Fix the design and send it again"), which the agent retries inside its turn and cost budget; a revise is `build_revise` the same way (D367). No other code path sent a shell to a model, and that one needs an agent loop with tools. **The seam** (`gregtec.build.shell_brief`, `gregtec.llm.designers`): one system text (the vocabulary in `build_request`'s own words, now one constant both use, and the gate's rules), one user text (the request words inside a `<request>` block, the theme and its rules, the sizes, the palette as `registry@meta` with catalog names, the layout's machines, keep-outs, corridors, chases and doorways, and for a revise the current operations), one JSON schema, one answer `{"ops": [...]}`. The candidate gets **no tools, no code execution, no file or world access** (the opposite of the reference mod, which runs model-written Python). The answer is parsed as data (at most 60,000 characters, as `build_request`'s `design`), the unused keys dropped (null, or `""` for the four text keys below), and handed to the product's own functions (`shell_store.make`, `shell_versions.revise`), so the gate is literally the same. **Repair rounds:** a refused answer is sent back with the gate's reasons at most **2** times, for every model alike, each recorded (judgement call: the product's bound is the agent's turn budget, which a tool-less call has none of; 2 keeps a run's worst case at three calls a step). Each round is one stateless request (the brief again plus the refused answer, clipped to 4,000 characters), never a conversation, so no vendor's history rules (thinking blocks, reasoning items) differ. **One schema for both vendors:** a flat operation object whose every key is required, `additionalProperties: false`, no numeric or array limits (OpenAI's strict mode wants every property required; Anthropic's rejects `minimum`/`maximum`): the gate checks values, not the schema. A key an operation does not use is null, except the four that name a role or a block (`role`, `block`, `edge`, `parapet_role`), which are plain strings and `""` when unused: Anthropic's structured outputs take at most **16** properties with a union type in one request and answer 400 above that (its "Schema complexity limits", read 2026-11-21), a nullable key is one, and all-nullable would be 17; this way there are 13, and a test counts them. **The same limits:** 16,000 output tokens a call (thinking or reasoning included, on both APIs), 600 s an answer, the same retry rule (a rate limit, 5xx, timeout, network failure or cut-off stream at most twice more; the server's `Retry-After` when given, never longer than 60 s, else 2 s and 8 s; a 401/403, a quota or spend-limit 429 and a 400/404 are not retried and that model is not asked again in the run). Effort is per model in `gos.toml` and unset means the vendor's own default, which differs per model (said in the runbook). Anthropic's server-side refusal fallback is **not** used (it would answer with another model). **Anthropic** goes through the `anthropic` SDK already in the repo (streamed, `output_config.format`, the SDK's retries off). **OpenAI** is plain HTTPS with the standard library, no new dependency: `POST /v1/responses` with `instructions`, `input`, `text.format` `json_schema` `strict`, `max_output_tokens`, optional `reasoning.effort`, `store: false`, `stream: true`, built from OpenAI's API reference, Structured Outputs, Error codes and Rate limits pages as read on 2026-11-20 (`developers.openai.com`). Model ids are not in code: on that day OpenAI's models page listed both names from the reference mod, `gpt-6-astra` ($10 in, $50 out, $1 cached input per million) and `gpt-5.6-sol` ($4, $20, $0.40; promotional until 2026-11-30); the jar proves nothing about either. **Keys:** a keyring entry `openai` (`gos key set openai`; `keys.OPTIONAL`, so the setup wizard does not ask for it), never in `gos.toml` (a key-like name in `[[bakeoff.models]]` is refused with the command to use), logs, reports or errors (API error text is scrubbed of the key and anything key-shaped). `api.openai.com` is contacted only by a bake-off run that names an OpenAI model. **Unchanged:** the live roles (`[models]`) stay Anthropic-only and priced by `gregtec.llm.prices`; game numbers come only from the DB and the planner; no model ranks routes; nothing here writes a world. | Refines D370's "a model bake-off against other vendors: not built" | Clayton, 2026-11-20 (the harness's relayed request); PR for WO-10.11; checklist 5.11.16, 5.11.17 |
| D375 | 2026-11-20 | A bake-off run: cases, where it is kept, blind lettering, rating, the report's order (WO-10.11 (D), D367, D368) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** `gos shell bakeoff run PLAN... --models A,B [--large PLAN,...]` (`gregtec.build.shell_bakeoff`). **Cases** are data (`tests/evals/shell_bakeoff.tsv`, six: a small and a large line, the two starter themes, one `tight` block limit, one with no words; each with one follow-up and D368's `expect`/`touches` checks). The plans are his saved ones (the fixture plans in CI): the small cases house `PLAN...`, the large ones `--large` (default the same). `tight` is 110% of GOS's own procedural shell's blocks for that case (a geometry count, no game number). **Per step** (a design, then its follow-up) it records: passed on the first try, repair rounds, why refused, blocks, palette entries used, the follow-up's asked/kept checks, seconds, tokens, cost. A gate-repaired design counts as passed (its status says repaired). **Kept apart:** `GOS_HOME/data/bakeoff/<run>/` holds `run.json` and, per case and blind letter, a small GOS_HOME of its own with that answer's builds and design file, written by the unchanged `shell_store` and `shell_versions`. Nothing goes into `data/build/shells` or `data/build/designs`, so a bake-off shell is never the current design, is not listed and cannot be exported (`gos shell export` reads the other folder). D367's "nothing is ever deleted" holds for his designs; a bake-off run is test data and `gos shell bakeoff delete RUN` removes the whole run and its report after saying what it removes. **Blind:** per case the models are shuffled with `random.Random` seeded with the run's seed and the case id and lettered A, B, C; the seed is stored in the run (and `--seed` sets it), so the lettering is reproducible. Folder names, version notes (`asked` is the case's words, `by` is `architect`, no cost), the progress lines, `gos shell bakeoff show`, the routes and the viewer carry the letter only; `run.json` alone knows who is who. **Rating:** per case a letter that has a design, `tie` or `bad` (none is good), changeable while blind. **Reveal:** `gos shell bakeoff report` (or the panel's button) names the models once every rateable case is rated, or earlier with `--reveal` (his choice, noted in the report); after that ratings are closed. **Report** (`data/reports/shell-bakeoff-<run>.md` and its table card): per model gate first-try and final pass counts, mean repair rounds, mean blocks, follow-up checks, calls, tokens, cost, seconds and his picks, then a per-case table with the refusal reasons. **Order of the model rows:** most cases picked, then most steps through the gate on the first try, then fewest repair rounds on average, then lowest cost (unknown last), then the label. It is a report's row order, not a route ranking, and it says that six cases are a first look, not proof. | - | PR for WO-10.11; checklist 5.11.18 |
| D376 | 2026-11-20 | Spending on a bake-off: the estimate, the yes, the caps, unpriced models (WO-10.11 (B), (D), D47, D48, D164, D334) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** Spending is his. **Models and prices** are his `gos.toml` (`[[bakeoff.models]]`: `label`, `provider` anthropic or openai, `model`, optional `effort`, optional `input_usd`, `output_usd`, `cached_input_usd`, `cache_write_usd` per million tokens; `[bakeoff] cap_usd`, default 10, and `cap_tokens`, default 1,000,000). An Anthropic model in `gregtec.llm.prices` is priced from that table; any other model is priced only from his numbers; **a model with no price shows its cost as unknown and is capped by tokens**, never guessed (cached input defaults to the input price and a cache write to 1.25 times it, so a missing detail never undercounts). The table is read only by the bake-off commands, so a mistake in it never stops the core. **Before a paid run** the command prints cases x models x the token bounds x the prices (a likely figure: every answer passes at once and uses a quarter of the output limit; and the worst case: every repair round at the whole limit), the hosts it contacts and the cap, and waits for a typed `yes` (`--yes` skips the question, never the caps). **The question states only money GOS can bound:** "Type yes to spend up to $X" is the most the priced models can cost (their worst case, or the cap if lower); a model without a price never gets a dollar figure, not even $0.00: the question reads "an unknown amount (label: no price, limited to N tokens, not to a dollar amount)", alone or after the priced models' figure, and a run with no priced model shows the token cap alone as its hard cap. It is refused, with nothing spent, while the AI is off (his switch or D48's credit pause: D164; the fake models still run), when a key is missing (it names `gos key set`), or once today's spend has reached `[budget] day_max_usd` (D334). **The day cap also bounds the run:** a run gets no higher a cap than the day cap still leaves, and the command says so before the question ("this run's cap is lowered from $10.00 to that"). The other paid evals refuse when their worst case does not fit the day; a bake-off has its own hard cap, so lowering it keeps the day cap just as safe and lets the full run follow the one-request run on the same day (refusing made the runbook's second run fail with the defaults, $10 and $10). **The cap is hard:** before every call the worst that call can cost (its input counted as one token a byte, the whole output limit, at the model's dearest input rate) is added to what is spent, and the run stops before a call that could pass the cap, with what it has: finished steps stay, the step in hand is dropped, nothing is half-written. Ctrl+C stops the same way. The check runs before every try, a retry too, and its input bound includes the schema and 1,000 tokens of framing. **A try that broke off after it was sent** (a timeout, a cut-off or oversized stream, an error inside a begun answer) reports no usage, yet the vendor may bill it: each is held against the cap at that same worst case (`run.json` `unmeasured`), is not in the cost column or `llm_usage` (nothing measured to record), and the run's last lines and the report say how many there were and the most they can have cost. A refusal before any answer began (401, 429, 5xx, no connection) holds nothing. Every real call is recorded in `llm_usage` (lane `bakeoff`, task `bakeoff:<run>`; an unpriced call with 0 USD and `:unpriced`), so the cycle, the alerts and the day count every priced call. An unpriced model's money is unknown to GOS, so the day cap and the alerts cannot see it: the token cap and the worded yes are its only gates, and the estimate says so on that model's line. Fake models are free and recorded nowhere. | - | PR for WO-10.11; checklist 3.13.11, 5.3.10 |
| D377 | 2026-11-20 | The bake-off panel in the Build view and its routes (WO-10.11 (D), plan 7.14, D92, D370) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton (board approval).** Under the shell workbench, shown only once a run exists: a run's cases as chips, the brief and the follow-up, the entries side by side as A, B, C in the workbench's own 3D viewer (layer slider, ghosts) with the first two designs' materials table, a switch between the first design and the version after the follow-up, the pick buttons (a letter, a tie, none is good), and **Name the models and show the report**, enabled once every case is rated, which then shows the report as a table card. Built only from the design system and the workbench's parts (panel, chips, buttons, the `bom` table, the table card); no new card type. **Runs are started only from the CLI** (judgement call: the estimate and the typed yes belong where he reads them, and it keeps the new UI small); giving up blind rating (`--reveal`) is CLI only too. **Routes:** `GET /api/shells/bakeoff`, `/{run}`, `/{run}/view`, `/{run}/compare` for admin, client and device; `POST /{run}/rate` and `/{run}/report` for admin and paired devices only, as the other shell routes. The blind view is built from a fixed list of fields, so before the report no route sends a model's label, id, provider, cost, token count or time. The reason a run stopped at a cap is one fixed sentence in every blind view (panel, routes, `show`, the run's last line): it names no model, no amount and not which cap, because the stop falls before one model's call in a case the other has answered; the sentence with the model in it is kept apart in `run.json` and shown in the report. No board yet: needs his approval (D92). | - | PR for WO-10.11; checklist 5.10.16 |
| D378 | 2026-11-21 | A bake-off model that can't be asked at all stops the run (WO-10.11 follow-up, D375, D376) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton.** Spending is his, and this is a choice about his money. **Before:** a model whose request failed in a way a retry can't fix (`gregtec.llm.designers.STOPS_MODEL`: the key refused with HTTP 401 or 403; the request refused with another 4xx such as a wrong model id, 400 or 404, or an effort value the model doesn't take; the account out of credit or at a spend limit) was not asked again, and the run carried on: that model showed "no design" under one letter of every case while every request of the other models was still paid for, and nothing said so before the report. A run like that has nothing to compare, so the money buys nothing. **Now:** the first such failure stops the run before the next call (`status` `stopped`). The step in hand is dropped; everything finished before stays and can be rated. The blind views (the run's last line, `show`, `list`, the panel, the routes) give one fixed sentence that names no model, no vendor and no error ("a model could not be asked at all (a refused key, a wrong model id or no credit), so nothing more was spent on the others"), because the stop can fall after the other model has answered the case and a name would tell the letters apart (as D377 does for a cap). The sentence with the model, its id and the vendor's own reason is kept apart in `run.json` (`why_named`) and shown by the report, which needs no rating when the run made nothing and `--reveal` (CLI only, D377) when he doesn't want to rate what it made. A redirect from OpenAI's address counts as such a failure (it is never followed, so the key reaches no other host). **Unchanged:** a passing failure (a rate limit, a server error, a timeout, a network failure, a cut-off answer) is retried as before (D374) and, if it stays, costs that one request its design; the caps, the estimate and the yes (D376). **The other way** (carry on with the remaining models) is kept as his choice in the checklist; it would be a `[bakeoff]` setting if he asks for it. | - | PR "Shell bake-off follow-ups" (fix/bakeoff-followups); checklist 5.3.11, 3.13.11 |
| D379 | 2026-11-22 | NEI lines with Thaumcraft aspect stacks are skipped and counted, not loaded (bug: `gos db build` on the full pack's run 20261122T170438Z, export contract NEI section) | **PM decision under D158, pending Clayton (gameplay: does he want Thaumcraft recipes in GOS at all?).** **Before:** a stack with neither `strId` nor `neiFluidName` refused the whole run, and Aspect Recipe Index (not in the test-harness pack) writes Thaumcraft aspects as `{TCAspect, Count, name}` in Alchemy (2,454 aspect stacks), Arcane Infusion (3,578), Aspect Combination (6) and Items Containing Aspect (6), so the first full-pack build stopped at `nei/Alchemy.jsonl:1`. **Now (option b):** `gregtec.ingest.nei.read_nei` skips every line that has a stack (an `anyOf` or `returns` entry too) with a non-empty `TCAspect`, before reading any of its stacks, so a skipped line names no item; it counts them per handler (`NeiRefs.aspect_lines`); the report prints `NEI: skipped N lines with Thaumcraft aspects (aspects aren't modelled, so their recipes aren't in the DB, D379): <handler> (n), ...` and `export_run.sources_json` keeps the total (`merge.aspect_lines`). Aspect Combination and Items Containing Aspect are lookup pages, not recipes, and are skipped the same way. Any other unknown stack shape (an empty `TCAspect` included) still refuses the run with its file and line. **Why not (a), an `aspect:<tag>` ref kind:** it needs schema version 8 (an aspect table and an `aspect_id` column on `recipe_input`, `recipe_input_alt` and `recipe_output`), and every planner reader of a recipe's inputs (graph, solve, ledger, sustain) to treat an aspect input as not producible by a GT route and flag it; a missed reader would make an infusion recipe look free. Dropping only the aspects would do the same (a recipe that looks cheaper than it is, which the planner could route through). Skipping loses those recipes' outputs as routes, which is safe: the planner never proposes a Thaumcraft step. **Later:** if Clayton wants Thaumcraft in GOS, model aspects as (a) in a WO of its own (TODO in `nei.py`). | - | PR for the aspect-stack fix; setup guide step 55 |
| D380 | 2026-11-22 | Starting the supervisor with Smart App Control on (D115 (6), D116, V35) | Smart App Control (On on Clayton's PC, found 2026-11-22 on release 7423123) blocks the release's unsigned `gos-supervisor.exe` and `pythonw.exe` every time (WinError 4551, CodeIntegrity event 3077) and allows `python.exe` and `gos.exe`. So GOS never starts those two. **`gos serve --detach` and `gos deploy`'s restart** always run `<venv>\Scripts\python.exe -P -m gregtec.supervisor [--config ABS]` (`servicecli.supervisor_argv`, one helper for both) with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW \| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, not `DETACHED_PROCESS`: the venv's python.exe is a launcher that starts the base python.exe, which with no console at all would open a visible one. Always, not as a fallback after a 4551: one path, the same everywhere, and no first failed launch to catch. `-P`: the start folder isn't on the import path, as it wasn't for the trampoline. A launch Windows still refuses prints one line, not a traceback. **Login start:** still the HKCU Run key (D116: no admin, no stored password, Clayton's interactive session, not elevated, so `GetLastInputInfo` works, V35; Startup apps can turn it off), now `"%SystemRoot%\System32\conhost.exe" --headless "<GOS_APP>\current\.venv\Scripts\python.exe" -P -m gregtec.supervisor --login`. conhost.exe is Windows' own, Microsoft-signed console host, and `--headless` gives python.exe a console with no window. Rejected: a Scheduled Task "At log on" (an interactive-token task still shows a console window for python.exe, and the hidden options run outside his desktop session, the reason D116 chose the Run key); `powershell -WindowStyle Hidden` (a window flashes); a VBScript/`wscript` wrapper (VBScript is being removed from Windows, and Smart App Control also judges scripts); our own signed wrapper (a code-signing certificate is spending, Clayton's call). `--headless` is undocumented, so the PR asks Clayton to confirm it on his PC. **`gos doctor`** prints Smart App Control's state (`HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\CI\Policy` `VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState`: 0 off, 1 on, 2 evaluation) and warns when the Run key still names `gos-supervisor.exe` or `pythonw.exe`. Clayton runs `install_login_start.ps1` again himself, from `GOS_APP\scripts` | D116's launcher (`gos-supervisor.exe --login`); `gos-supervisor.exe` in D115 (6)'s restart and in `gos serve --detach` | Clayton's PC, 2026-11-22; this PR |

A group of fake recipes only (GT's assembly-line map) is unchanged. Rejected: `fake` in the identity hash (it would give each recipe a second key and change every key of the assembly-line map, which chain templates cite); 39 allow-list rows (two keys for one recipe). **(2) Industrial Chisel (2 of 4, the voidstone pillars):** one recipe of each pair is a GT line, the other an NEI line (no amperage, no flags) loaded as NEI-only in the map's own namespace. GT++'s chisel writes its recipes with `category` null, so NEI's handler `gt.recipe.industrialchisel` was not a filled GT category. `merge.merge` now compares an NEI line whose handler is a GT map with uncategorised recipes against those recipes with no category (both merge steps). NEI-only chisel lines that were silent copies of GT's (different alternatives, so a different identity) are merged or dropped as GT recipes NEI shows in another form. GT's chisel recipes are all fake, so the planner still doesn't route through the Industrial Chisel (decided below). **(3) Genuine duplicates, allow-listed (11):** 6 compressor, 2 assembler, 1 forge hammer and 2 TecTech Star Kernel groups whose two recipes differ only in EU/t or duration (the curation task's diff), one recipe registered twice, like the harness's packager rows. **(4) Industrial Chisel glass (2), allow-listed:** the dump lines (idx 221/222 and 1766/1767) are one fake display recipe written twice (owner `gregteclink`: GT++ built them lazily while the export ran), equal in every field but the accepted set: the second adds `chisel:glass2@1` and `Natura:NetherGlass@0/@1` to the glass carving group. Lazily built display recipes may pair up differently on another export; a new pair shows as a new curation row. **Decided:** (a) the planner keeps leaving the Industrial Chisel's fake recipes out, as every fake recipe but the assembly line's: chiselling only changes a block's look, a hand chisel does it for nothing, so the machine adds no GT route, and admitting it would change the planner for no gain (checklist 5.1.21 keeps the other way open). (b) The chisel map stays keyed as now, with the allow-list for pairs, not put in `ALT_IDENTITY_MAPS`: keying a slot by its accepted set would give each lazily built recipe a key from a set that can itself differ between exports, so keys would be no stabler, and every chisel citation key would change. A pair that shows up later is one checked curation row. | D385 | 2026-11-22 | Freeform shell designs: a sandboxed Python build script, and volumes and hip/shed/sawtooth roofs in the vocabulary (WO-10.12, plan 7.10, D316, D317, D374; the CC0 "AI Building" mod as reference) | **Clayton, 2026-11-22** (after the free bake-off, before the paid one): he wants buildings that are dynamic and interesting, not "build, almost Lego-style", and pointed at the CC0 Fabric mod *AI Building* 1.0.0 (Minecraft 26.2), whose model writes Python with the `mcschematic` library that the mod runs unsandboxed with the player's own Python. Offered (A) a build language GOS interprets itself, (B) the model's real Python in a sandbox, (C) raw block data, he chose **(B)**, and asked whether the same would help the power and recipe designers. **Answer to that: no** (said to him in the session): their output is game numbers and rankings, which come from `gregtec.planner`/`gregtec.db` and `planner/rank.py` only (CLAUDE.md, plan 0.1 principle 1, 7.2); a model's code could only recompute or invent them, and layouts are hard-constraint packing the solver does exactly. So the exception is **shell geometry only**. **What changed.** (1) `shell.OPS` gains `script` (`{"op": "script", "script": "<python>"}`, at most one a design): it rides inside the ops list, so the store, versions, build ids (the script text is hashed with the ops), undo, compare, the viewer, export's rebuild and the bake-off's blind views work unchanged; the reviser's `describe` says a script changed by its line count, never its text; GOS's own reviser edits the other ops and leaves a script as it is. (2) `gregtec.build.sandbox` runs it: a syntax-tree check (imports of math, random, itertools, functools only; no class, with, global, nonlocal, async; no name starting `__`, no attribute starting `_`; none of open, eval, exec, compile, getattr, setattr, type, object, format, ...; at most 40,000 characters), then `sys.executable -S -s -B -P sandbox_child.py` (on Windows the base interpreter, `sys._base_executable`, not the venv's launcher, which would start a second process, D380; stdlib only) from an empty temporary folder with an environment of PYTHONHASHSEED=0 and the UTF-8 switches only (SYSTEMROOT on Windows), the script on stdin; the child gives the script trimmed builtins and its building API (`set_block`, `fill`, `clear`, `get_block`; the facts as `BOUNDS`, `INTERIOR`, `WALL_TOP`, `ROOF_Y`, `HEADROOM`, `MAX_BLOCKS`, `PALETTE`, `ROLES`, `DOORWAYS`, `KEEP_CLEAR`), seeds `random`, and installs a PEP 578 audit hook that refuses every audited event after the script starts except the one exec of its code (files, sockets, processes, imports, ctypes, code objects, `os.system`); OS limits: a Windows Job Object (one process, 1 GiB, 8 CPU seconds, kill on close, every UI restriction), POSIX rlimits (1 GiB address space, 8 CPU seconds, no file may be written, 16 handles, no core), and a 10 s wall clock under the tools' 30 s deadline; the answer is JSON cells (at most 4 x max_blocks), read as data, resolved to palette roles or `registry@meta` and then the **unchanged** gate (D317). Tests show the classic escapes (`__subclasses__` to `os._wrap_close`, `BuiltinImporter`, `random._os`, an API function's `__globals__`) are refused by the syntax check and, with that check skipped, stopped by the audit hook with no file written. **Hardened after an adversarial review** (37 agents, 15 findings confirmed, all fixed): before the script runs the child removes every function from the modules that reach the OS (os, posix, nt, _signal, resource, _winapi, msvcrt, ...) and from the import machinery (_imp, importlib's frozen modules: they could make a fresh posix), because some calls raise no audit event (pidfd_send_signal could signal GOS or a Minecraft server; mkfifo/mknod could plant files); GOS reads the child's pipes with a cap (a flood kills it); on Windows the child is started out of GOS's own job where allowed, and nested without UI limits where not; a volume is clamped to the bounding volume (a huge one hung the gate); a saved build's script is replayed with 25 s; role numbers are ASCII digits; the reviser's flat roof drops a shed's side and a sawtooth's tooth, says it can't make a script-drawn shell taller, and doesn't add a parapet above a volume roof's ceiling; the eval's walls.top ignores tower walls; a repair round echoes up to 16,000 characters so a script comes back whole; the prompt's example keeps its vault inside the default headroom and says a script sees only its own blocks. **Residual risk, said plainly:** CPython's audit hooks are not a security boundary by design, and nothing here blocks the network at the OS level (no firewall rule, no AppContainer); the syntax check is the main wall and the hook and OS limits the backstop. A stronger Windows wall (a separate low-rights account or AppContainer for the child) is a follow-up if he wants it. (3) The vocabulary also gets what the earlier, op-only half of this WO started: `walls`, `pillars`, `windows` and `roof` take an optional volume (`lo`, `hi`: their ring and heights, a roof's ceiling) for towers, clerestories, tiers and cross gables, and `roof` gains `hip` (a mansard when it meets its ceiling), `shed` and `sawtooth` (`sides` the high side, `edge` the upright face, `every` the tooth). (4) The designer's prompt (`shell_brief.system_prompt`) is rewritten freeform-first: design principles (no plain box; silhouette, roofline, more than one mass; base, body and top; rhythm and contrast from the palette's roles; an entrance that reads; the process inside within the theme), the script API with a worked example that a test runs through the gate, the unchanged rules ("stay valid first, then be ambitious"); about 6,800 characters, up from 2,235 (about 1,200 more input tokens a call). `PROMPT_VERSION` 2; every bake-off run records `prompt` (version, a hash of the text, its size) and the report shows it, a run without it reads as v1. (5) `fake-ornate` adds a script (corner buttresses; round stacks over a smelting line, a domed cupola otherwise; nothing on a tight block limit, read from `MAX_BLOCKS`); the six cases are unchanged and the free bake-off still gives fake-sloppy 0 of 12 first try, 12 of 12 passed. **Not done (asks):** facade depth beyond the one-block wall ring (projecting bays, overhanging cornices, buttresses standing out) needs a larger bounding volume, which changes the footprint a pasted shell claims in his world; the procedural (No-AI) generator still draws the op vocabulary; his mod's in-game parts (ghost preview, guided block-by-block build, reference image) are a separate WO for GregtecLink on 1.7.10. CLAUDE.md, plan 4.6 and Appendix A carry the exception | Narrows D316's "the model gets no code execution; it sends data" and D374's "no tools, no code execution" to: no code-execution *tool*, and one sandboxed build script per shell design; D317's gate is unchanged | `claude/fervent-ride-f8niry` |
| D386 | 2026-11-22 | Facades that stand out (a relief zone around the wall ring) and a 7th, striking bake-off case (WO-10.13, D316, D375, D385) | **Clayton, 2026-11-22**, answering D385's questions: "Don't worry about practicality. I'll be using WorldEdit to spawn everything in. Facades absolutely can stick out. Sure, add the 7th bake-off case", and the free (procedural) generator is being phased out in favour of the AI designers, so it gets nothing new; "the quicker we can [get to testing], the better". **Relief:** `shell.LIMITS["relief"]` (default 3, 0-8): the bounding volume reaches `relief` blocks past the one-block wall ring on every side, from the ground to the top. `Spec.walls` is the wall line (the old bounding volume); `walls`, `pillars`, `windows`, `roof` and `lights` without a volume work on it, so every design from before draws the same cells; `inset` may be negative down to `-relief` (a ring that steps out). Below the roof a block may go in the wall ring or the relief zone, never the layout's interior; above `wall_top`, anywhere in the bounding volume (eaves and cornices that overhang). The doorways run from the entrance out to the bounding volume's edge, so nothing in the relief zone blocks the way in. Scripts read `WALLS` and `RELIEF`; the brief states the wall line and the relief. A saved build or version from before D386 is rebuilt, viewed, exported and revised with relief 0 (its record has none), and relief 0 adds nothing to the build id, so its id and size are unchanged. The `.schematic` covers the whole bounding volume, relief zone included: a `//paste` also pastes air there (it clears 3 blocks around the building; `//paste -a` keeps what stands there). No tool, CLI or workbench option for relief yet (the default applies everywhere): one to add when he asks. **Prompt v3** (`PROMPT_VERSION` 3, 7,381 characters): the relief zone, facade depth among the principles ("buttresses, projecting bays, a porch, a stepped plinth, a cornice that overhangs"), "the shell is pasted whole with WorldEdit, so detail costs nothing to build: spend the blocks", and an example whose buttresses stand out. **The 7th case** (`tests/evals/shell_bakeoff.tsv`): an optional last column `headroom` (`default` or 1-16; a file without it still loads), the first six unchanged with `default`; `landmark-large` (large line, th-foundry, smelting, headroom 10): "a striking landmark over the line and its power: a skyline of towers and chimney stacks, a dramatic roofline, and deep facades whose buttresses, bays and cornices stand out from the walls"; follow-up "no lights" (`lights=none`, touches lights and script: the deterministic checks read ops, so a follow-up a script-drawn building can be checked on was chosen). A run records each case's headroom. Shape helpers in the sandbox: `line`, `ring`, `box`, `cylinder`, `sphere` (`dome=True`), `pyramid`, `arch`, each through the same cell caps. Prompt v4 (`PROMPT_VERSION` 4, 6,952 characters): the ambition first (choose the building's idea; massing, roofline, facades with depth, rhythm, curves, the entrance; "never a box with windows"; pasted with WorldEdit, so be generous), the theme's massing, roof, window and adapt notes marked "a suggestion" in the brief and in the prompt, the space, the answer and the API, helper fragments instead of a whole building, the unchanged rules. **Cases** (`tests/evals/shell_bakeoff.tsv`, replacing the six plus one; he asked for variety over comparability): foundry-stacks (th-foundry, headroom 12), marble-temple (th-marble-works, 10), gothic-cathedral, desert-ziggurat, pagoda-workshop and deco-powerhouse (theme `none`, a vanilla palette of their own in the new `palette` column, headroom 12 to 16), marble-theme-only (no words, 10); relief 3 to 6 (`relief` column); follow-ups GOS's own reviser understands, checked with the new `changed` (the design changed) or `lights=none`. **Measured with one real call** (approved by him; this session's OpenAI connection): GPT-6 Astra, effort high, gothic-cathedral: accepted, 3,515 blocks, nothing removed; a steep nave roof, twin spired towers, buttresses with pinnacles, stained glass; 13,184 output tokens and about 3,600 input, about $0.70. So `LIKELY_OUTPUT` (the likely answer's share of the output limit) goes from 0.25 to 0.8: the estimate for the seven is likely about $3.78 (Opus 5.5) and $9.45 (Astra), above the default $10 cap and day limit; the guide says how to raise both for a run that finishes. | D388 | 2026-11-22 | The bake-off's window looked hung and its panel lagged the PC: a line before each answer, a 3D view that doesn't redraw for nothing (WO-10.14 follow-up, D377, D387) | **Clayton, 2026-11-22**, during his first full v4 run: "it appears to be getting stuck and lagging really, really bad ... I can't click which one I prefer. The terminal looks like it's hung up ... when I swap to the Marble Temple, nothing displays." **Causes:** (1) a v4 answer is a building program the model reasons about first: about 4 minutes in the one real call (D387), and the run asks 28 answers in turn, so one and a half to two hours; the window printed a line only when an answer was done, so long silences looked like a hang, and a case not reached yet (Marble Temple) has nothing to show; (2) the shell viewer draws every visible face as an SVG polygon, and v4 buildings are 3,000+ blocks: thousands of polygons per view, two views side by side, and the panel re-read the run every 5 s and re-rendered both scenes, and every hover rebuilt every polygon to mark one block, which lagged the browser and with it the PC. **Fix:** the runner says `<case> <letter> design: asking (an answer can take several minutes)` and `<case> <letter> follow-up: asking` before each answer (letters only: blind); the viewer's faces are built once per drawing (`useMemo`), the hovered block's faces are marked in place (a class toggled on its polygons, not a rebuild), a drag turns the view at most once an animation frame, and `ShellScene3D` is memoised on its build and source key, so the panel's refresh no longer redraws a scene. | D389 | 2026-11-22 | An open interior, machine arrangements per case, and a run that says it is still working (WO-10.15, D312, D316, D387, D388) | **Clayton, 2026-11-22**, before the next v4 run: "the layout of the machines inside is apparently immutable ... just two rows ... The building designer could have a little bit more design freedom if it just moved those buildings around"; and "it's not hung. It's just taking a really long time. Maybe some more diagnostics in the printout ... and obviously, errors if it runs out of credits". **What constrained it** (read from the code): the layout planner (D312) puts every machine in rows along corridor spines, at most `max_run` long, flush against each other; and the shell gate (D316) kept the layout's whole bounding rectangle empty below the roof, so every building was a ring around one rectangle however the machines stood. **Not done: the model moving machines.** A machine's place is the planner's: its reach (the controller's front and every hatch from the entrance), its corridor and chase, and the services' routes are checked there, and a model's positions would need that whole check redone per answer; the arrangement stays deterministic and the planner's. **Done:** (1) the planner gets `spacing` (0-8: open floor between machines in a row) and `band_gap` (0-16: between bands), deterministic like the rest; with `max_run` they give long halls, compact blocks or spread-out floors; both are left out of the layout JSON and id while 0, so every layout from before keeps its id. (2) **An open interior** (`Spec.interior_open`, the default for every new design): the gate keeps empty only what the machines need (their keep-outs, the corridors, the chases, the doorways), so the floor between machines is the shell's: columns, inner walls, arcades, light wells, courtyards, and outer walls that step in and out around the machines. The planner's service space is the corridors and the keep-out rings and the runs go through the chases, so nothing a machine needs is given up. A build or version from before D389 is rebuilt, exported and revised with the interior closed (its record has no `interior_open`), and its build id is unchanged; export re-lays out a build with the layout options it was saved with. (3) Prompt v5 (`PROMPT_VERSION` 5): the space described that way; the brief says whether the interior is open. (4) The bake-off's case file gets an optional `layout` column (the planner's options); the seven cases each have their own (`max_run` 16-48, `spacing` 0-4, `band_gap` 0-6), so they don't wrap the same rows. (5) The runner says `... still waiting for the answer (2m 00s)` every minute while a model thinks, how long each answer took, a refused answer's reason, and after each request (never after one letter: the prices would tell the models apart) what the run has spent of its cap; an account out of credit already stopped the run at once with a `Stopped:` line (D378), and the guide now says so | Narrows D316's closed interior for new designs; extends D312's options and D387's cases | `claude/fervent-ride-f8niry` |
| D388 | 2026-11-22 | Why the MV steel plan isn't buildable, and two preference-free fixes (WO-3.10, D176, D189, D198, `docs/reviews/WO-3.10_steel_route.md`) | **Pending Clayton (PR questions).** Clayton, 2026-11-22: the steel plan uses an ore from another planet, detours (into gears and back into ingots) and is badly overbuilt.
